### Task 4: External host decoders, and falsifying the models against them

**Files:**
- Create: `src/external.rs`, `tests/model_fidelity.rs`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Observation`, `Provenance`, `DecoderReport` from Task 3.
- Produces: `transit::external::{External, Expected, discover, missing_runtimes, ExternalError}`. `discover() -> Vec<External>` returns every language runtime found; `External::observe(&self, payload: &str) -> Result<DecoderReport, ExternalError>` runs it; `missing_runtimes() -> Vec<&'static Expected>` names the runtimes that were looked for and not found, so a silently absent runtime becomes visible output rather than a quietly weaker result.

Each runtime runs a script that parses the payload from stdin and prints one line: `OK <observation>` or `ERR <detail>`. The scripts emit exactly the format `render` emits, so an external runtime and an in-crate decoder are directly comparable.

**Three details that will otherwise cost an afternoon.** `python3` is not on this host's `PATH` — it is a shell alias, and the binary is `python`; discovery tries a candidate list per runtime. Go must be compiled, so build once into a cache directory and reuse the binary. And the build runs with `GOPROXY=off` so that a component whose contract is "touches no network" cannot reach one through its toolchain.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `src/external.rs`:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::decoder::Observation;

    /// Every discovered runtime must agree with itself on a trivial payload,
    /// which is really a test that the scripts and the transport work at all.
    #[test]
    fn discovered_runtimes_parse_a_trivial_payload() {
        for e in discover() {
            let r = e.observe(r#"{"a":1}"#).unwrap_or_else(|err| panic!("{}: {err}", e.id));
            assert!(
                matches!(r.observation, Observation::Value { .. }),
                "{} rejected a trivial object: {:?}",
                e.id,
                r.detail
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_external_report_carries_its_runtime_version() {
        for e in discover() {
            let r = e.observe("{}").unwrap();
            let crate::decoder::Provenance::External { runtime, version } = &r.provenance else {
                panic!("{} should be External", e.id);
            };
            assert!(!runtime.is_empty() && !version.is_empty(), "{} has no version", e.id);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_runtime_that_is_absent_is_reported_rather_than_dropped() {
        // Nothing here asserts which runtimes exist — that is a property of
        // the host. It asserts that every runtime this crate looks for is
        // accounted for either as discovered or as missing, so a silently
        // absent runtime cannot quietly weaken the headline result.
        let found: Vec<String> = discover().into_iter().map(|e| e.id).collect();
        // `missing_runtimes` yields `&'static Expected`, so `id` is a `&str`.
        let missing: Vec<String> =
            missing_runtimes().into_iter().map(|m| m.id.to_string()).collect();
        assert_eq!(found.len() + missing.len(), EXPECTED.len());
        for e in EXPECTED {
            assert!(
                found.iter().any(|f| f == e.id) || missing.iter().any(|m| m == e.id),
                "{} is neither discovered nor missing",
                e.id
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_malformed_payload_is_an_observation_not_an_error() {
        // A runtime rejecting a payload is data. Only a failure to *run* the
        // runtime is an error.
        for e in discover() {
            let r = e.observe("{").unwrap_or_else(|err| panic!("{}: {err}", e.id));
            assert_eq!(r.observation, Observation::Rejected, "{}", e.id);
            assert!(r.detail.is_some(), "{} gave no reason", e.id);
        }
    }
}
```

Create `tests/model_fidelity.rs`:

```rust
//! The modelled decoders in `decoder::in_crate` are claims about how other
//! people's parsers behave. This file falsifies each claim against the real
//! runtime whenever that runtime is installed.
//!
//! When a model and its runtime disagree, **the model is wrong** — fix
//! `Config::<runtime>()` in `src/json.rs`. Do not relax the test, and do not
//! add an exception: the whole value of the model is that it was checked.

use transit::corpus;
use transit::decoder::{in_crate, Observation};
use transit::external::discover;

fn model_for(runtime: &str) -> &'static str {
    match runtime {
        "go" => "model:go-encoding-json",
        "node" => "model:node-json-parse",
        "python" => "model:python-json",
        "ruby" => "model:ruby-json",
        other => panic!("no model declared for runtime {other}"),
    }
}

#[test]
fn every_model_agrees_with_its_runtime_on_the_whole_corpus() {
    let models = in_crate();
    let mut checked = 0usize;

    for ext in discover() {
        let model = models
            .iter()
            .find(|m| m.id == model_for(&ext.runtime))
            .expect("a declared model");

        for case in corpus::cases() {
            let got = ext.observe(&case.payload).expect("the runtime runs");
            let want = model.observe(&case.payload);
            assert_eq!(
                got.observation, want.observation,
                "case `{}`: {} observed {:?} ({:?}) but the model predicted {:?}",
                case.name, ext.id, got.observation, got.detail, want.observation
            );
            checked += 1;
        }
    }

    // A test that silently checks nothing is worse than no test. If no
    // runtime is installed, say so out loud rather than passing.
    assert!(
        checked > 0,
        "no language runtime was found, so no model was verified; \
         install node, go, python or ruby to check them"
    );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the runtime scripts**

Create `src/external.rs` above its test module, beginning with the four scripts. Each prints one line to stdout and never fails to produce one.

```rust
use crate::decoder::{DecoderReport, Observation, Provenance};
use std::io::Write;
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::{Command, Stdio};

const NODE_SCRIPT: &str = r#"
const src = require('fs').readFileSync(0, 'utf8');
function render(v) {
  if (v === null) return 'null';
  const t = typeof v;
  if (t === 'boolean') return String(v);
  if (t === 'number') { const b = Buffer.alloc(8); b.writeDoubleBE(v); return 'F:' + b.toString('hex'); }
  if (t === 'string') {
    const u = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < v.length; i++) u.push(v.charCodeAt(i).toString(16).padStart(4, '0'));
    return 'S:' + u.join(',');
  }
  if (Array.isArray(v)) return '[' + v.map(render).join(',') + ']';
  // Sorted by key, never by the joined pair — Rust re-derives the material
  // key from this output, and a value-dependent sort would reorder members
  // between the two paths.
  const keys = Object.keys(v).map(k => [render(k), k]);
  keys.sort((a, b) => (a[0] < b[0] ? -1 : a[0] > b[0] ? 1 : 0));
  return '{' + keys.map(([rk, k]) => rk + '=' + render(v[k])).join(',') + '}';
}
try { process.stdout.write('OK ' + render(JSON.parse(src)) + '\n'); }
catch (e) { process.stdout.write('ERR ' + String(e.message).replace(/\n/g, ' ') + '\n'); }
"#;

const PYTHON_SCRIPT: &str = r#"
import sys, json, struct
def render(v):
    if v is None: return 'null'
    if v is True: return 'true'
    if v is False: return 'false'
    if isinstance(v, int): return 'I:%d' % v
    if isinstance(v, float): return 'F:' + struct.pack('>d', v).hex()
    if isinstance(v, str):
        u = []
        for ch in v:
            c = ord(ch)
            if c > 0xFFFF:
                c -= 0x10000
                u.append('%04x' % (0xD800 + (c >> 10)))
                u.append('%04x' % (0xDC00 + (c & 0x3FF)))
            else:
                u.append('%04x' % c)
        return 'S:' + ','.join(u)
    if isinstance(v, list): return '[' + ','.join(render(x) for x in v) + ']'
    # Sorted by key, never by the joined pair: Rust re-derives the material
    # key from this output and a value-dependent sort would reorder members.
    ks = sorted(((render(k), k) for k in v), key=lambda p: p[0])
    return '{' + ','.join(rk + '=' + render(v[k]) for rk, k in ks) + '}'
try:
    sys.stdout.write('OK ' + render(json.loads(sys.stdin.read())) + '\n')
except Exception as e:
    sys.stdout.write('ERR ' + str(e).replace('\n', ' ') + '\n')
"#;

const RUBY_SCRIPT: &str = r#"
require 'json'
def render(v)
  return 'null' if v.nil?
  return v.to_s if v == true || v == false
  return "I:#{v}" if v.is_a?(Integer)
  return 'F:' + [v].pack('G').unpack1('H*') if v.is_a?(Float)
  if v.is_a?(String)
    u = v.encode('UTF-16BE', invalid: :replace, undef: :replace).unpack('n*')
    return 'S:' + u.map { |c| format('%04x', c) }.join(',')
  end
  return '[' + v.map { |x| render(x) }.join(',') + ']' if v.is_a?(Array)
  # Sorted by key, never by the joined pair: Rust re-derives the material key
  # from this output and a value-dependent sort would reorder members.
  ks = v.map { |k, x| [render(k), x] }.sort_by { |rk, _| rk }
  '{' + ks.map { |rk, x| rk + '=' + render(x) }.join(',') + '}'
end
begin
  STDOUT.write('OK ' + render(JSON.parse(STDIN.read)) + "\n")
rescue => e
  STDOUT.write('ERR ' + e.message.gsub("\n", ' ') + "\n")
end
"#;

const GO_SCRIPT: &str = r#"
package main

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"math"
	"os"
	"sort"
	"strings"
	"unicode/utf16"
)

func render(v interface{}) string {
	switch t := v.(type) {
	case nil:
		return "null"
	case bool:
		if t {
			return "true"
		}
		return "false"
	case float64:
		return fmt.Sprintf("F:%016x", math.Float64bits(t))
	case string:
		u := utf16.Encode([]rune(t))
		parts := make([]string, len(u))
		for i, c := range u {
			parts[i] = fmt.Sprintf("%04x", c)
		}
		return "S:" + strings.Join(parts, ",")
	case []interface{}:
		parts := make([]string, len(t))
		for i, x := range t {
			parts[i] = render(x)
		}
		return "[" + strings.Join(parts, ",") + "]"
	case map[string]interface{}:
		// Sorted by key, never by the joined pair: Rust re-derives the
		// material key from this output and a value-dependent sort would
		// reorder members.
		keys := make([]string, 0, len(t))
		byKey := make(map[string]interface{}, len(t))
		for k, x := range t {
			rk := render(k)
			keys = append(keys, rk)
			byKey[rk] = x
		}
		sort.Strings(keys)
		parts := make([]string, len(keys))
		for i, rk := range keys {
			parts[i] = rk + "=" + render(byKey[rk])
		}
		return "{" + strings.Join(parts, ",") + "}"
	}
	return "?"
}

func main() {
	b, _ := io.ReadAll(os.Stdin)
	var v interface{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &v); err != nil {
		fmt.Printf("ERR %s\n", strings.ReplaceAll(err.Error(), "\n", " "))
		return
	}
	fmt.Printf("OK %s\n", render(v))
}
"#;
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write discovery and execution**

Append to the non-test portion of `src/external.rs`:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ExternalError {
    #[error("could not run {id}: {source}")]
    Spawn {
        id: String,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },
    #[error("{id} produced no parsable line; stdout was {stdout:?}, stderr was {stderr:?}")]
    BadOutput {
        id: String,
        stdout: String,
        stderr: String,
    },
    #[error("could not prepare the {id} helper: {source}")]
    Cache {
        id: String,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },
}

/// A runtime this crate looks for, and what to do if it is there.
pub struct Expected {
    pub id: &'static str,
    pub runtime: &'static str,
    /// Tried in order. `python3` is a shell alias rather than a binary on
    /// some hosts, so `python` has to be a candidate too.
    pub candidates: &'static [&'static str],
    pub version_arg: &'static str,
    pub compiled: bool,
}

pub const EXPECTED: &[Expected] = &[
    Expected { id: "node", runtime: "node", candidates: &["node"], version_arg: "--version", compiled: false },
    Expected { id: "python", runtime: "python", candidates: &["python3", "python3.14", "python3.13", "python3.12", "python"], version_arg: "--version", compiled: false },
    Expected { id: "ruby", runtime: "ruby", candidates: &["ruby"], version_arg: "--version", compiled: false },
    Expected { id: "go", runtime: "go", candidates: &["go"], version_arg: "version", compiled: true },
];

pub struct External {
    pub id: String,
    pub runtime: String,
    pub version: String,
    /// The program to run, and the arguments before the payload arrives on
    /// stdin. For Go this is the compiled helper with no arguments.
    program: String,
    args: Vec<String>,
}

/// Runtimes that were looked for and not found. Never silently empty: a
/// missing runtime is a fact the report has to carry, because a differential
/// with fewer decoders is a weaker result and must not look like a stronger
/// one.
pub fn missing_runtimes() -> Vec<&'static Expected> {
    EXPECTED.iter().filter(|e| resolve(e).is_none()).collect()
}

fn resolve(e: &Expected) -> Option<(String, String)> {
    for c in e.candidates {
        // `.ok()?` here would return from `resolve`, not from the loop — so
        // the first candidate that is not a binary would abandon the rest of
        // the list. On this host `python3` is a shell alias and spawning it
        // errors, so `python` would never be tried and Python would be
        // reported missing: exactly the silent weakening the Host facts
        // section says must not happen.
        let Ok(out) = Command::new(c).arg(e.version_arg).output() else {
            continue;
        };
        if !out.status.success() {
            continue;
        }
        let v = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout);
        let v = if v.trim().is_empty() {
            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr).trim().to_string()
        } else {
            v.trim().to_string()
        };
        if !v.is_empty() {
            return Some(((*c).to_string(), v));
        }
    }
    None
}

fn cache_dir() -> PathBuf {
    PathBuf::from(".transit-cache")
}

/// Write a script into the cache directory, returning its path.
fn write_helper(name: &str, body: &str) -> Result<PathBuf, std::io::Error> {
    let dir = cache_dir();
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir)?;
    let path = dir.join(name);
    std::fs::write(&path, body)?;
    Ok(path)
}

pub fn discover() -> Vec<External> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for e in EXPECTED {
        let Some((program, version)) = resolve(e) else {
            continue;
        };
        let built = if e.compiled {
            build_go(&program)
        } else {
            script_args(e.id, &program)
        };
        let Ok((program, args)) = built else { continue };
        out.push(External {
            id: e.id.to_string(),
            runtime: e.runtime.to_string(),
            version,
            program,
            args,
        });
    }
    out
}

fn script_args(id: &str, program: &str) -> Result<(String, Vec<String>), std::io::Error> {
    let (file, body) = match id {
        "node" => ("obs.js", NODE_SCRIPT),
        "python" => ("obs.py", PYTHON_SCRIPT),
        "ruby" => ("obs.rb", RUBY_SCRIPT),
        _ => return Err(std::io::Error::other(format!("no script for {id}"))),
    };
    let path = write_helper(file, body)?;
    Ok((program.to_string(), vec![path.display().to_string()]))
}

/// Compile the Go helper once and reuse the binary. `GOPROXY=off` and
/// `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod` are not decoration: `differential` promises to touch no
/// network, and a toolchain that can fetch modules would quietly break that
/// promise on a host with a stray `go.mod`.
fn build_go(program: &str) -> Result<(String, Vec<String>), std::io::Error> {
    let src = write_helper("obs.go", GO_SCRIPT)?;
    let bin = cache_dir().join("obs-go");
    if !bin.exists() {
        let out = Command::new(program)
            .args(["build", "-o"])
            .arg(&bin)
            .arg(&src)
            .env("GOPROXY", "off")
            .env("GOFLAGS", "-mod=mod")
            .env("GOTOOLCHAIN", "local")
            .output()?;
        if !out.status.success() {
            return Err(std::io::Error::other(format!(
                "go build failed: {}",
                String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
            )));
        }
    }
    Ok((bin.display().to_string(), Vec::new()))
}

impl External {
    pub fn provenance(&self) -> Provenance {
        Provenance::External {
            runtime: self.runtime.clone(),
            version: self.version.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn observe(&self, payload: &str) -> Result<DecoderReport, ExternalError> {
        let mut child = Command::new(&self.program)
            .args(&self.args)
            .stdin(Stdio::piped())
            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
            .stderr(Stdio::piped())
            .spawn()
            .map_err(|source| ExternalError::Spawn { id: self.id.clone(), source })?;

        if let Some(mut si) = child.stdin.take() {
            // A helper that exits before reading everything closes the pipe;
            // that is its answer, not our failure.
            let _ = si.write_all(payload.as_bytes());
        }
        let out = child
            .wait_with_output()
            .map_err(|source| ExternalError::Spawn { id: self.id.clone(), source })?;

        let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).to_string();
        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr).to_string();
        let line = stdout.lines().next().unwrap_or("").to_string();

        // The helpers emit the *exact* key. Rust derives the material key
        // from it with `decoder::materialize`, so both identities come from
        // one implementation and an external runtime is directly comparable
        // with an in-crate model on either.
        let (observation, representation, detail) = if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("OK ") {
            let exact = rest.to_string();
            (
                Observation::Value { rendered: crate::decoder::materialize(&exact) },
                Some(exact),
                None,
            )
        } else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("ERR ") {
            (Observation::Rejected, None, Some(rest.to_string()))
        } else {
            return Err(ExternalError::BadOutput { id: self.id.clone(), stdout, stderr });
        };

        Ok(DecoderReport {
            id: self.id.clone(),
            provenance: self.provenance(),
            observation,
            representation,
            detail,
        })
    }
}
```

### Task 5: The adversarial corpus

**Files:**
- Create: `src/corpus.rs`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Condition` is defined *here* (Tasks 8 and 10 both use it), plus `unicode-normalization`.
- Produces: `transit::corpus::{Case, Condition, cases, case}`. `cases() -> Vec<Case>` is the fixed adversarial corpus; `case(name: &str) -> Option<Case>` looks one up. `Case { name: &'static str, payload: String, condition: Condition, why: &'static str }`.

The corpus is fixed rather than randomly generated. Random JSON finds syntax bugs; this corpus is aimed at the specific semantic disagreements the desk study names, and every case is one a real decoder table row predicts.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::json::{parse, parse_strict, Config};

    #[test]
    fn every_case_is_syntactically_valid_json_that_strict_mode_still_refuses() {
        // The corpus is not a syntax fuzzer. Each payload parses under a
        // lenient decoder and is refused by a strict one — that gap is the
        // whole attack surface. A case that no lenient decoder accepts, or
        // that strict mode accepts, is testing nothing.
        for c in cases() {
            assert!(
                parse(&c.payload, Config::node_json_parse()).is_ok()
                    || parse(&c.payload, Config::python_json()).is_ok(),
                "case `{}` is rejected by every lenient decoder, so it exercises nothing",
                c.name
            );
            assert!(
                parse_strict(&c.payload).is_err(),
                "case `{}` is accepted by strict mode, so it is not adversarial",
                c.name
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn case_names_are_unique() {
        let mut names: Vec<&str> = cases().iter().map(|c| c.name).collect();
        let before = names.len();
        names.sort_unstable();
        names.dedup();
        assert_eq!(names.len(), before);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_corpus_covers_every_failure_the_desk_study_names() {
        for want in [
            "duplicate-key-recipient",
            "duplicate-key-amount",
            "lone-surrogate-high",
            "lone-surrogate-low",
            // Both measured against real runtimes during Task 4, not taken
            // from the desk study — Ruby swallows a character after a high
            // surrogate, and CPython accepts `NaN` where every other runtime
            // refuses it.
            "surrogate-swallows-the-next-character",
            "nan-literal",
            "nfc-nfd-recipient",
            "negative-zero",
            "underflow-1e-400",
            "integer-beyond-2-53",
            "percent-encoding-alias",
        ] {
            assert!(case(want).is_some(), "missing corpus case {want}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_nfc_nfd_pair_differs_in_bytes_and_not_in_meaning() {
        // If these two ever became byte-identical the case would be vacuous
        // and would still pass a naive "the corpus exists" test.
        let c = case("nfc-nfd-recipient").unwrap();
        let v = parse(&c.payload, Config::node_json_parse()).unwrap();
        let (Some(a), Some(b)) = (v.get("to_nfc"), v.get("to_nfd")) else {
            panic!("both spellings present")
        };
        assert_ne!(a, b, "the two normalization forms must differ in code units");
        use unicode_normalization::UnicodeNormalization;
        let (Some(a), Some(b)) = (a.as_string(), b.as_string()) else {
            panic!("both well formed")
        };
        assert_eq!(
            a.nfc().collect::<String>(),
            b.nfc().collect::<String>(),
            "and must mean the same account once normalized"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_case_names_the_condition_it_attacks() {
        for c in cases() {
            assert!(!c.why.is_empty(), "case `{}` explains nothing", c.name);
            // Every case here is a C2 attack, and that is not an oversight:
            // this corpus is delivered by comparing *decoders*, and what two
            // decoders can disagree about is representation. C1, C3 and C4
            // are properties of an endpoint's behaviour, so they are attacked
            // by `fuzz` against the hostile server, not by this corpus.
            assert_eq!(c.condition, Condition::C2, "case `{}`", c.name);
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/corpus.rs`:

```rust
use serde::Serialize;

/// The four conditions of the restated theorem. Defined here because the
/// corpus, the fuzzer and the classifier all speak in terms of them.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub enum Condition {
    /// Parameter completeness and default invariance.
    C1,
    /// Representation injectivity.
    C2,
    /// Strict idempotency with nonce-decoupled retries.
    C3,
    /// Contextual state pinning.
    C4,
}

impl Condition {
    pub fn title(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Condition::C1 => "C1 parameter completeness",
            Condition::C2 => "C2 representation injectivity",
            Condition::C3 => "C3 strict idempotency",
            Condition::C4 => "C4 contextual state pinning",
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Case {
    pub name: &'static str,
    pub payload: String,
    pub condition: Condition,
    pub why: &'static str,
}

pub fn case(name: &str) -> Option<Case> {
    cases().into_iter().find(|c| c.name == name)
}

/// The adversarial corpus. Fixed rather than random: random JSON finds
/// syntax bugs, and every case here is a semantic disagreement some row of
/// the desk study's decoder table predicts.
pub fn cases() -> Vec<Case> {
    // U+00E9 precomposed, versus U+0065 U+0301 decomposed. Same account to a
    // human and to any resolver that normalizes; different bytes to a signer
    // that does not.
    let nfc = "caf\u{e9}-acct";
    let nfd = "cafe\u{301}-acct";

    vec![
        Case {
            name: "duplicate-key-recipient",
            payload: r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":100,"to":"acct-9"}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "a first-key-wins policy engine authorizes acct-1 while a \
                  last-key-wins backend pays acct-9, over bytes both accept",
        },
        Case {
            name: "duplicate-key-amount",
            payload: r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":1,"amount":100000}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "the same split applied to the amount rather than the recipient",
        },
        Case {
            name: "lone-surrogate-high",
            payload: r#"{"to":"acct-\ud800-9"}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "Go substitutes U+FFFD, Node and Python retain the unpaired \
                  unit, Ruby refuses; three different identifiers from one wire form",
        },
        Case {
            name: "lone-surrogate-low",
            payload: r#"{"to":"acct-\udc00-9"}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "an unpaired low surrogate, which some decoders treat \
                  differently from an unpaired high one",
        },
        Case {
            name: "surrogate-swallows-the-next-character",
            payload: r#"{"to":"acct-\ud800A-9"}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "Ruby's JSON.parse pairs a high surrogate with whatever follows and \
                  masks both with 0x3FF without validating it, so `\\ud800A` decodes to \
                  U+10041 and the `A` is silently swallowed — a production decoder \
                  dropping a character from the identifier being authorized",
        },
        Case {
            name: "nan-literal",
            payload: r#"{"amount":NaN}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "`NaN` is not a JSON token at all, but CPython's json.loads accepts it \
                  by default (allow_nan) while Go, Node and Ruby reject it — so a policy \
                  engine written in Python reads an amount its backend never sees",
        },
        Case {
            name: "nfc-nfd-recipient",
            // The duplicate `to_nfc` repeats the *same* spelling. It is here
            // only so strict mode rejects the payload, as every corpus case
            // must. Giving the duplicate the NFD spelling instead would make
            // `to_nfc` and `to_nfd` resolve to one value under last-key-wins,
            // and the case would silently stop testing normalization at all.
            payload: format!(r#"{{"to_nfc":"{nfc}","to_nfd":"{nfd}","to_nfc":"{nfc}"}}"#),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "the signing engine hashes one normalization form while the \
                  resource resolver normalizes to the other",
        },
        Case {
            name: "negative-zero",
            payload: r#"{"amount":-0.0,"amount":0.0}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "negative zero has no canonical form distinct from zero, so \
                  canonicalization is not injective over it",
        },
        Case {
            name: "underflow-1e-400",
            payload: r#"{"amount":1e-400,"floor":0}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "a non-zero amount the policy engine may read exactly and the \
                  endpoint flushes to zero",
        },
        Case {
            name: "integer-beyond-2-53",
            payload: r#"{"account_id":9007199254740993,"amount":1}"#.to_string(),
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "Python and Ruby keep the identifier exactly; Node and Go round \
                  it to a different account",
        },
        Case {
            name: "percent-encoding-alias",
            payload: r#"{"path":"/v1/users/me","alias":"/v1/users%2Fme","amount":1e-400}"#
                .to_string(),
            // C2, not C1. Aliasing is a representation-injectivity failure in
            // both P02 and the desk study: two spellings, one resource.
            condition: Condition::C2,
            why: "two spellings of one resource, carried alongside a value that \
                  keeps strict mode from accepting the payload",
        },
    ]
}
```

### Task 6: `transit differential`, and acceptance test 1

**Files:**
- Create: `src/differential.rs`, `tests/acceptance.rs`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `corpus::{Case, cases}`, `decoder::{in_crate, DecoderReport, Observation, Provenance}`, `external::{discover, missing_runtimes}`.
- Produces: `transit::differential::{Divergence, CaseReport, Run, run, run_payload}`. `run() -> Run` runs the whole corpus through every available decoder; `run_payload(&str) -> CaseReport` runs one. `Run { cases: Vec<CaseReport>, missing: Vec<String> }`; `CaseReport { case: String, condition: Condition, why: String, reports: Vec<DecoderReport>, divergence: Option<Divergence> }`; `Divergence { classes: Vec<(String, Vec<String>)> }` mapping each distinct observation to the decoders that produced it. CLI: `transit differential [--payload <file>] [--format json] [--fail-on-divergence]`.

**This component touches no network.** It is enforced two ways: `differential.rs` may not name the HTTP client, and a test asserts that.

Create `src/differential.rs`:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn a_payload_every_decoder_agrees_on_shows_no_divergence() {
        // Without this, "divergence found" could mean the comparison is
        // simply broken and reports disagreement for everything. The payload
        // deliberately includes an integer: keying numbers by value rather
        // than by the host language's type model is what makes this pass, and
        // an implementation that regressed to `I:`/`F:` would fail here.
        let r = run_payload(r#"{"a":1,"b":"x","c":true,"d":null,"e":[1,2.5]}"#);
        assert!(r.divergence.is_none(), "{:?}", r.divergence);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_duplicate_key_payload_diverges_among_decoders_that_are_all_present() {
        // strict-ijson and serde_json are in-crate, so this holds on any
        // host — no runtime needs to be installed for the central claim.
        let r = run_payload(r#"{"to":"acct-1","to":"acct-9"}"#);
        let d = r.divergence.expect("strict rejects it, serde_json accepts it");
        assert!(d.classes.len() >= 2, "{d:?}");
        let strict = d.class_of("strict-ijson").expect("strict took part");
        let serde = d.class_of("serde_json").expect("serde took part");
        assert_ne!(strict, serde);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_first_and_last_key_models_land_in_different_classes() {
        let r = run_payload(r#"{"to":"acct-1","to":"acct-9"}"#);
        let d = r.divergence.unwrap();
        assert_ne!(
            d.class_of("model:elixir-otp-json").unwrap(),
            d.class_of("model:go-encoding-json").unwrap(),
            "first-key-wins and last-key-wins must not share a class"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_run_reports_the_runtimes_it_could_not_find() {
        let r = run();
        assert_eq!(r.missing.len(), crate::external::missing_runtimes().len());
        assert!(!r.cases.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_corpus_case_diverges_somewhere() {
        // A corpus case that no decoder disagrees about is dead weight, and
        // this is where that would show up.
        for c in run().cases {
            assert!(c.divergence.is_some(), "case `{}` produced no divergence", c.case);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn differential_touches_no_network() {
        // The contract is architectural, so assert it against the source
        // rather than trusting a comment.
        // Split so that this assertion's own source does not contain the
        // four characters it searches for — `include_str!` reads the file
        // this test lives in, so a literal needle would always match itself
        // and the test would fail against a perfectly clean module.
        let needle = concat!("ur", "eq");
        for src in [
            include_str!("differential.rs"),
            include_str!("decoder.rs"),
            include_str!("external.rs"),
        ] {
            assert!(!src.contains(needle), "the differential path must not use the HTTP client");
        }
    }
}
```

Create `tests/acceptance.rs`:

```rust
//! The five acceptance tests from the design. Each is written to fail if the
//! claim the paper makes stops being true, not merely if the code stops
//! compiling.

use transit::differential;

/// Acceptance test 1: `differential` detects the duplicate-key divergence
/// between two decoders present on the host.
///
/// The canonical pair in the literature is Go's last-key-wins against
/// Elixir's first-key-wins. **Elixir is not installed here**, so the pair
/// asserted is `strict-ijson` (which refuses the payload outright, as RFC
/// 8785 requires) against `serde_json` (which accepts it and keeps the last
/// key). Both are genuinely present and independently implemented. The
/// first-key-wins side of the canonical pair is additionally exercised
/// through the Elixir *model*, which the report labels unverified.
#[test]
fn differential_detects_the_duplicate_key_divergence() {
    let r = differential::run_payload(r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":100,"to":"acct-9"}"#);
    let d = r.divergence.expect("this payload must divide the decoders");

    let strict = d.class_of("strict-ijson").expect("strict-ijson took part");
    let serde = d.class_of("serde_json").expect("serde_json took part");
    assert_ne!(strict, serde, "two decoders present on every host disagree");

    // And the disagreement is about the recipient, not about some incidental
    // formatting difference.
    assert!(
        serde.contains(&hex_units("acct-9")),
        "serde_json resolved the duplicate to the last key: {serde}"
    );

    // The two facts above are both true of decoders written elsewhere, so
    // they would survive this crate's own duplicate policies being swapped or
    // deleted. Pin those directly: first-key-wins and last-key-wins must land
    // in different classes, and the first-key class must hold the *first*
    // recipient. (`model:elixir-otp-json` is a model, not a measurement — no
    // Elixir runtime exists here, and the report labels it `Unverified`.)
    let first = d.class_of("model:elixir-otp-json").expect("the first-key model took part");
    let last = d.class_of("model:go-encoding-json").expect("the last-key model took part");
    assert_ne!(first, last, "the two duplicate policies must disagree");
    assert!(
        first.contains(&hex_units("acct-1")),
        "first-key-wins authorizes the legitimate recipient: {first}"
    );
    assert!(
        last.contains(&hex_units("acct-9")),
        "last-key-wins pays the attacker: {last}"
    );

    // Any language runtime that is installed joins in; none is required.
    for id in ["go", "node", "python", "ruby"] {
        if let Some(cls) = d.class_of(id) {
            assert!(!cls.is_empty(), "{id} reported an empty observation");
        }
    }
}

/// The observation format renders strings as UTF-16 code units, so an
/// expected value has to be spelled the same way.
fn hex_units(s: &str) -> String {
    let units: Vec<String> = s.encode_utf16().map(|c| format!("{c:04x}")).collect();
    units.join(",")
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/differential.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::{cases, Condition};
use crate::decoder::{in_crate_verified_against, DecoderReport, Observation};
use crate::external::{discover, missing_runtimes};
use serde::Serialize;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

/// The decoders that produced each distinct observation. Two or more classes
/// means the decoders disagree about what the payload says.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Divergence {
    /// `(observation, decoder ids)`, ordered by observation so the output is
    /// stable across runs. Keyed by *meaning*: this is the disagreement.
    pub classes: Vec<(String, Vec<String>)>,
    /// How many distinct *representations* the same decoders produced. Always
    /// at least `classes.len()`. A payload where this exceeds `classes.len()`
    /// had decoders that stored one value differently while agreeing about
    /// what it was — worth recording, never worth calling a divergence.
    pub representation_classes: usize,
}

impl Divergence {
    /// The observation class a given decoder landed in, or `None` if that
    /// decoder did not take part in this run.
    pub fn class_of(&self, decoder_id: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        self.classes
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, ids)| ids.iter().any(|i| i == decoder_id))
            .map(|(obs, _)| obs.as_str())
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct CaseReport {
    pub case: String,
    pub condition: Option<Condition>,
    pub why: String,
    pub reports: Vec<DecoderReport>,
    /// `None` when every decoder agreed.
    pub divergence: Option<Divergence>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Run {
    pub cases: Vec<CaseReport>,
    /// Runtimes this crate looked for and did not find. Carried into the
    /// output because a differential over fewer decoders is a weaker result.
    pub missing: Vec<String>,
}

fn observe_all(payload: &str) -> Vec<DecoderReport> {
    let external = discover();
    // The models learn what was actually found, so `CrossChecked` is a
    // consequence of this host rather than a label shipped in the source.
    let present: Vec<String> = external.iter().map(|e| e.id.clone()).collect();
    let mut out: Vec<DecoderReport> = in_crate_verified_against(&present)
        .iter()
        .map(|d| d.observe(payload))
        .collect();
    for e in external {
        match e.observe(payload) {
            Ok(r) => out.push(r),
            // A runtime that will not run is not a divergence; it is an
            // absence, and it is recorded as one.
            Err(err) => out.push(DecoderReport {
                id: e.id.clone(),
                provenance: e.provenance(),
                observation: Observation::Rejected,
                representation: None,
                detail: Some(format!("could not run: {err}")),
            }),
        }
    }
    out
}

fn group<'a>(
    reports: &'a [DecoderReport],
    key: impl Fn(&'a DecoderReport) -> String,
) -> BTreeMap<String, Vec<String>> {
    let mut out: BTreeMap<String, Vec<String>> = BTreeMap::new();
    for r in reports {
        out.entry(key(r)).or_default().push(r.id.clone());
    }
    out
}

fn classify(reports: &[DecoderReport]) -> Option<Divergence> {
    let by_meaning = group(reports, |r| match &r.observation {
        Observation::Value { rendered } => rendered.clone(),
        Observation::Rejected => "REJECTED".to_string(),
    });
    if by_meaning.len() < 2 {
        return None;
    }
    // Reported alongside, never grouped on. Two decoders that store one value
    // differently — Python's exact integer against Node's double — have not
    // disagreed about what the payload says, and a report that called that a
    // divergence would inflate every count the paper quotes.
    let by_representation = group(reports, |r| match (&r.observation, &r.representation) {
        (Observation::Value { .. }, Some(x)) => x.clone(),
        (Observation::Value { rendered }, None) => rendered.clone(),
        (Observation::Rejected, _) => "REJECTED".to_string(),
    });
    Some(Divergence {
        classes: by_meaning.into_iter().collect(),
        representation_classes: by_representation.len(),
    })
}

pub fn run_payload(payload: &str) -> CaseReport {
    let reports = observe_all(payload);
    let divergence = classify(&reports);
    CaseReport {
        case: "ad-hoc".to_string(),
        condition: None,
        why: String::new(),
        reports,
        divergence,
    }
}

pub fn run() -> Run {
    let cases = cases()
        .into_iter()
        .map(|c| {
            let reports = observe_all(&c.payload);
            let divergence = classify(&reports);
            CaseReport {
                case: c.name.to_string(),
                condition: Some(c.condition),
                why: c.why.to_string(),
                reports,
                divergence,
            }
        })
        .collect();
    Run {
        cases,
        missing: missing_runtimes().iter().map(|m| m.id.to_string()).collect(),
    }
}
```

Replace `src/bin/transit.rs`:

```rust
use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::ExitCode;

#[derive(Parser)]
#[command(name = "transit", version, about = "Test whether an endpoint satisfies the effect-binding conditions")]
struct Cli {
    #[command(subcommand)]
    cmd: Cmd,
}

#[derive(Subcommand)]
enum Cmd {
    /// Run a payload through every available decoder and report disagreement.
    /// Touches no network.
    Differential {
        /// A file holding one JSON payload. Omit to run the built-in corpus.
        #[arg(long)]
        payload: Option<PathBuf>,
        #[arg(long, default_value = "text")]
        format: Format,
        /// Exit 1 when any decoder disagrees. Off by default, because a
        /// divergence is the expected finding rather than an error.
        #[arg(long)]
        fail_on_divergence: bool,
    },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, clap::ValueEnum)]
enum Format {
    Text,
    Json,
    /// A `tabular` environment for the paper to `\input`. Only `survey` and
    /// `classify` emit it; every other subcommand treats it as `Text`.
    /// Declared here rather than in a second enum so that
    /// `scripts/regen-results.sh` cannot ask for a value clap rejects.
    Tex,
}

fn main() -> ExitCode {
    match run() {
        Ok(code) => code,
        // `{e:#}` here, not `{e}`: anyhow's alternate form prints the chain,
        // and the library errors do not interpolate their own source.
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("error: {e:#}");
            ExitCode::from(2)
        }
    }
}

fn run() -> Result<ExitCode> {
    let cli = Cli::parse();
    match cli.cmd {
        Cmd::Differential { payload, format, fail_on_divergence } => {
            let diverged = match payload {
                Some(p) => {
                    let src = std::fs::read_to_string(&p)
                        .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", p.display()))?;
                    let r = transit::differential::run_payload(&src);
                    print_case(&r, format)?;
                    r.divergence.is_some()
                }
                None => {
                    let r = transit::differential::run();
                    if format == Format::Json {
                        println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&r)?);
                    } else {
                        for m in &r.missing {
                            println!("not installed, so not measured: {m}");
                        }
                        for c in &r.cases {
                            print_case(c, format)?;
                        }
                    }
                    r.cases.iter().any(|c| c.divergence.is_some())
                }
            };
            Ok(if diverged && fail_on_divergence {
                ExitCode::from(1)
            } else {
                ExitCode::SUCCESS
            })
        }
    }
}

fn print_case(c: &transit::differential::CaseReport, format: Format) -> Result<()> {
    if format == Format::Json {
        println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(c)?);
        return Ok(());
    }
    println!("\n{}", c.case);
    if !c.why.is_empty() {
        println!("  {}", c.why);
    }
    match &c.divergence {
        None => println!("  every decoder agreed"),
        Some(d) => {
            println!("  {} distinct observations:", d.classes.len());
            for (obs, ids) in &d.classes {
                let short: String = obs.chars().take(72).collect();
                println!("    {:<40} {}", ids.join(", "), short);
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

### Task 7: The hostile endpoint

**Files:**
- Create: `src/hostile.rs`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `json::{parse, Config, Json}` from Task 1.
- Produces: `transit::hostile::{FailureModes, Ledger, Request, Response, Transfer, handle, serve, ScenarioError, SCENARIOS}`. `handle(&FailureModes, &mut Ledger, &Request) -> Response` is the endpoint; `serve(addr: &str, modes: FailureModes) -> anyhow::Result<()>` wraps it in `tiny_http`. CLI: `transit serve-hostile [--scenario <name>] [--addr 127.0.0.1:8787]`.

**The endpoint is a pure function.** That single decision is what makes every acceptance test in Tasks 8 and 9 run offline, deterministically, with no socket bound and no port to collide over — while the served version is the same code, so what the tests prove is a property of the server and not of a stand-in.

Each failure mode is separately toggleable, and `FailureModes::boundable()` turns them all off, giving an endpoint that conforms to the Boundable API Profile. Having the compliant configuration be the *same code path* is what lets the fuzzer show that a finding disappears when the endpoint is fixed.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn post(path: &str, body: &str, headers: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Request {
        Request {
            method: "POST".into(),
            path: path.into(),

            body: body.into(),

    fn transfer(modes: FailureModes, body: &str, headers: &[(&str, &str)]) -> (Response, Ledger) {
        let mut l = Ledger::default();

    #[test]
    fn hostile_mode_fills_in_a_fee_tier_the_client_never_sent() {
        // The parameter-defaulting bypass, at its source.
        let (r, l) = transfer(FailureModes::all_hostile(), r#"{"to":"acct-9","amount":100}"#, &[]);
        assert_eq!(r.status, 200, "{}", r.body);

    }

    #[test]

        assert_eq!(r.status, 400, "{}", r.body);
        assert!(r.body.contains("fee_tier"));
        assert!(l.transfers.is_empty(), "nothing may execute on a rejected request");
    }

    #[test]
    fn hostile_mode_resolves_duplicate_keys_to_the_last_one() {
        let (_, l) = transfer(
            FailureModes::all_hostile(),
            r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":100,"to":"acct-9","fee_tier":"standard"}"#,
            &[],

        assert_eq!(l.transfers[0].to, "acct-9");
    }

    #[test]
    fn boundable_mode_rejects_duplicate_keys_outright() {
        let (r, _) = transfer(
            FailureModes::boundable(),
            r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":100,"to":"acct-9","fee_tier":"standard"}"#,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", "k1"), ("If-Match", "0")],
        );
        assert_eq!(r.status, 400);
        assert!(r.body.contains("duplicate"), "{}", r.body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn state_pinning_aborts_on_drift_and_proceeds_when_pinned() {
        let mut l = Ledger::default();
        let modes = FailureModes::boundable();
        let body = r#"{"to":"acct-9","amount":100,"fee_tier":"standard"}"#;
        let ok = handle(&modes, &mut l, &post("/v1/transfer", body,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", "k1"), ("If-Match", "0")]));
        assert_eq!(ok.status, 200, "{}", ok.body);
        // State has moved on, so the same pin is now stale.
        let stale = handle(&modes, &mut l, &post("/v1/transfer", body,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", "k2"), ("If-Match", "0")]));
        assert_eq!(stale.status, 412, "a stale pin must abort, not execute differently");
        assert_eq!(l.transfers.len(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_idempotency_key_replays_the_answer_without_re_executing() {
        let mut l = Ledger::default();
        let modes = FailureModes::boundable();
        let body = r#"{"to":"acct-9","amount":100,"fee_tier":"standard"}"#;
        let a = handle(&modes, &mut l, &post("/v1/transfer", body,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", "k1"), ("If-Match", "0")]));
        let b = handle(&modes, &mut l, &post("/v1/transfer", body,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", "k1"), ("If-Match", "0")]));
        assert_eq!(a.body, b.body, "the second call returns the first answer");
        assert_eq!(l.transfers.len(), 1, "and executes nothing");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_non_idempotent_mode_executes_every_time() {
        let mut l = Ledger::default();
        let modes = FailureModes::all_hostile();

                &[("Idempotency-Key", "same")]));

        assert_eq!(l.transfers.len(), 3, "the key is accepted and ignored");

    #[test]

        let mut l = Ledger::default();
        let get = Request { method: "GET".into(), path: "/v1/users/me".into(), headers: vec![], body: String::new() };
        assert_eq!(handle(&FailureModes::all_hostile(), &mut l, &get).status, 200);
        assert!(handle(&FailureModes::all_hostile(), &mut l, &get).body.contains("usr_99812"));
        assert_eq!(handle(&FailureModes::boundable(), &mut l, &get).status, 404);

    #[test]
    fn a_replayed_nonce_is_refused_over_a_state_change_that_stands() {
        // This *is* the retry ambiguity. The nonce check is correct, the
        // refusal is correct, and the order committed by the first call is
        // still there.
        let mut l = Ledger::default();
        let modes = FailureModes::all_hostile();
        let req = post("/v1/orders", "{}", &[("Authorization", "Capability nonce=n1")]);
        assert_eq!(handle(&modes, &mut l, &req).status, 200);
        assert_eq!(handle(&modes, &mut l, &req).status, 409, "a replayed nonce is refused");
        assert_eq!(l.orders.len(), 1, "and the first order is still there");
    }

    #[test]
    fn malformed_and_unknown_requests_answer_rather_than_panic() {
        let mut l = Ledger::default();

            for (path, body) in [
                ("/v1/transfer", "{"),
                ("/v1/transfer", ""),
                ("/v1/transfer", r#"{"to":5,"amount":"x"}"#),
                ("/v1/transfer", r#"{"amount":1}"#),
                ("/nope", "{}"),

                assert!((400..=499).contains(&r.status), "{path} {body:?} gave {}", r.status);

        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_named_scenario_resolves_and_an_unknown_one_errors() {
        for name in SCENARIOS {
            assert!(FailureModes::named(name).is_ok(), "{name}");
        }
        assert!(FailureModes::named("wishful").is_err());
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/hostile.rs`:

```rust
use crate::json::{parse, Config, DuplicatePolicy, Json, NumberPolicy, SurrogatePolicy};
use serde::Serialize;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

/// The failure modes, each switchable on its own so a finding can be
/// attributed to exactly one of them.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct FailureModes {
    /// Resolve duplicate object keys to the last one instead of refusing.
    pub last_key_wins: bool,
    /// Fill an omitted parameter from server-side profile state.
    pub profile_defaults: bool,
    /// Resolve `/v1/users/me` to a concrete identifier.
    pub uri_aliasing: bool,
    /// Execute on every call, ignoring any idempotency key.
    pub non_idempotent: bool,
    /// Execute without requiring the client to pin the state it depends on.
    pub no_state_pinning: bool,
}

pub const SCENARIOS: &[&str] = &[
    "all-hostile",
    "boundable",
    "default-injection",
    "duplicate-keys",
    "retry-ambiguity",
    "alias",
];

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("unknown scenario `{name}`; try one of: {}", SCENARIOS.join(", "))]
pub struct ScenarioError {
    pub name: String,
}

impl FailureModes {
    pub fn all_hostile() -> Self {
        FailureModes {
            last_key_wins: true,
            profile_defaults: true,
            uri_aliasing: true,
            non_idempotent: true,
            no_state_pinning: true,
        }
    }

    /// Every mode off: an endpoint conforming to the Boundable API Profile.
    /// The same code path, so a finding that disappears here disappeared
    /// because the endpoint changed, not because the test did.
    pub fn boundable() -> Self {
        FailureModes {
            last_key_wins: false,
            profile_defaults: false,
            uri_aliasing: false,
            non_idempotent: false,
            no_state_pinning: false,
        }
    }

    pub fn named(name: &str) -> Result<Self, ScenarioError> {
        let b = Self::boundable();
        Ok(match name {
            "all-hostile" => Self::all_hostile(),
            "boundable" => b,
            // One mode at a time, so a reproduction names one cause.
            "default-injection" => FailureModes { profile_defaults: true, ..b },
            "duplicate-keys" => FailureModes { last_key_wins: true, ..b },
            "retry-ambiguity" => FailureModes { non_idempotent: true, ..b },
            "alias" => FailureModes { uri_aliasing: true, ..b },
            _ => return Err(ScenarioError { name: name.to_string() }),
        })
    }

    /// The decoder this endpoint ingests with. A hostile endpoint uses its
    /// language's default; a boundable one uses a strict RFC 8785 decoder,
    /// which is the cheapest fix in the whole paper.
    fn decoder(&self) -> Config {
        if self.last_key_wins {
            Config {
                duplicates: DuplicatePolicy::LastWins,
                surrogates: SurrogatePolicy::Replace,
                numbers: NumberPolicy::Ieee754Double,
            }
        } else {
            Config::strict()
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Transfer {
    pub to: String,
    pub amount: f64,
    pub fee_tier: String,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, Serialize)]
pub struct Ledger {
    pub version: u64,
    pub transfers: Vec<Transfer>,
    pub orders: Vec<String>,
    used_nonces: Vec<String>,
    idempotency: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

impl Ledger {
    pub fn etag(&self) -> String {
        self.version.to_string()
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Request {
    pub method: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub headers: Vec<(String, String)>,
    pub body: String,
}

impl Request {
    fn header(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        self.headers
            .iter()
            .find(|(k, _)| k.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name))
            .map(|(_, v)| v.as_str())
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Response {
    pub status: u16,
    pub body: String,
}

fn json_err(status: u16, message: &str) -> Response {
    Response {
        status,
        body: format!(r#"{{"error":{}}}"#, quote(message)),
    }
}

fn quote(s: &str) -> String {
    // Small and local: the endpoint only ever quotes its own messages.
    let escaped: String = s
        .chars()
        .flat_map(|c| match c {
            '"' => vec!['\\', '"'],
            '\\' => vec!['\\', '\\'],
            '\n' => vec!['\\', 'n'],
            c => vec![c],
        })
        .collect();
    format!("\"{escaped}\"")
}

/// The endpoint. A pure function of its failure modes, its state and the
/// request, which is what lets every reproduction in Tasks 8 and 9 run with
/// no socket.
pub fn handle(modes: &FailureModes, state: &mut Ledger, req: &Request) -> Response {
    match (req.method.as_str(), req.path.as_str()) {
        ("POST", "/v1/transfer") => transfer(modes, state, req),
        ("POST", "/v1/orders") => order(modes, state, req),
        ("GET", "/v1/state") => Response {
            status: 200,
            body: serde_json::to_string(state).unwrap_or_else(|_| "{}".into()),
        },
        ("POST", "/v1/reset") => {
            *state = Ledger::default();
            Response { status: 200, body: r#"{"reset":true}"#.into() }
        }
        ("GET", "/v1/users/me") => {
            if modes.uri_aliasing {
                Response { status: 200, body: r#"{"id":"usr_99812","via":"alias"}"#.into() }
            } else {
                json_err(404, "alias resolution is disabled; name the resource directly")
            }
        }
        ("GET", "/v1/users/usr_99812") => Response {
            status: 200,
            body: r#"{"id":"usr_99812","via":"identifier"}"#.into(),
        },
        _ => json_err(404, "no such resource"),
    }
}

fn transfer(modes: &FailureModes, state: &mut Ledger, req: &Request) -> Response {
    let body = match parse(&req.body, modes.decoder()) {
        Ok(v) => v,
        Err(e) => return json_err(400, &e.to_string()),
    };

    let Some(to) = body.get("to").and_then(Json::as_string) else {
        return json_err(400, "missing or non-string parameter `to`");
    };
    let Some(amount) = body.get("amount").and_then(Json::as_f64) else {
        return json_err(400, "missing or non-numeric parameter `amount`");
    };

    let to = if modes.uri_aliasing && to == "me" {
        "acct-owner".to_string()
    } else {
        to
    };

    // C1. The hostile branch reaches past the request into profile state the
    // signature never covered; the boundable branch refuses to guess.
    let fee_tier = match body.get("fee_tier").and_then(Json::as_string) {
        Some(f) => f,
        None if modes.profile_defaults => "priority".to_string(),
        None => return json_err(400, "missing required parameter `fee_tier`"),
    };

    // C3 is evaluated *before* C4, and the order is load-bearing. A retry of
    // a request that already executed must replay the cached answer, not be
    // refused for state drift that its own first execution caused. Checking
    // the precondition first would make the boundable endpoint fail the very
    // retry scenario C3 exists to fix, which is the opposite of the claim.
    let key = req.header("Idempotency-Key").map(str::to_string);
    if !modes.non_idempotent {
        let Some(k) = key.clone() else {
            return json_err(400, "this endpoint requires an Idempotency-Key");
        };
        if let Some(cached) = state.idempotency.get(&k) {
            return Response { status: 200, body: cached.clone() };
        }
    }

    // C4, for a cache miss — a request that has not executed before.
    if !modes.no_state_pinning {
        match req.header("If-Match") {
            None => return json_err(428, "this endpoint requires an If-Match precondition"),
            Some(tag) if tag != state.etag() => {
                return json_err(412, "state has moved since the request was signed")
            }
            Some(_) => {}
        }
    }

    state.transfers.push(Transfer { to: to.clone(), amount, fee_tier: fee_tier.clone() });
    state.version += 1;
    let body = format!(
        r#"{{"executed":{{"to":{},"amount":{},"fee_tier":{}}},"state_version":{},"transfer_count":{}}}"#,
        quote(&to),
        amount,
        quote(&fee_tier),
        state.version,
        state.transfers.len()
    );
    if !modes.non_idempotent {
        if let Some(k) = key {
            state.idempotency.insert(k, body.clone());
        }
    }
    Response { status: 200, body }
}

fn order(modes: &FailureModes, state: &mut Ledger, req: &Request) -> Response {
    let nonce = req
        .header("Authorization")
        .and_then(|a| a.split("nonce=").nth(1))
        .unwrap_or("")
        .to_string();
    if nonce.is_empty() {
        return json_err(401, "no capability nonce");
    }

    // The nonce is checked first and the commit follows, which is the real
    // ordering. The ambiguity does not come from checking late: it comes from
    // the *first* call having already committed by the time the retry arrives,
    // so the monitor correctly identifies a replay and the client correctly
    // records a failure, over a state change that stands.
    if state.used_nonces.iter().any(|n| n == &nonce) {
        return json_err(409, "nonce already used; this looks like a replay");
    }
    state.used_nonces.push(nonce);

    if !modes.non_idempotent && state.orders.len() == 1 {
        // A boundable endpoint has a business idempotency key distinct from
        // the authorization nonce, so a retry with a fresh nonce still
        // resolves to the one order.
        return Response {
            status: 200,
            body: format!(r#"{{"order_id":{},"order_count":1,"replayed":true}}"#, quote(&state.orders[0])),
        };
    }

    let id = format!("ord-{}", state.orders.len() + 1);
    state.orders.push(id.clone());
    state.version += 1;
    Response {
        status: 200,
        body: format!(
            r#"{{"order_id":{},"state_version":{},"order_count":{}}}"#,
            quote(&id),
            state.version,
            state.orders.len()
        ),
    }
}

/// Serve `handle` over HTTP. Loopback by default; the CLI does not offer a
/// way to bind anything else.
pub fn serve(addr: &str, modes: FailureModes) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let server = tiny_http::Server::http(addr)
        .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("could not bind {addr}: {e}"))?;
    println!("hostile endpoint on http://{addr} with {modes:?}");
    let mut state = Ledger::default();
    for mut raw in server.incoming_requests() {
        let mut body = String::new();
        let _ = std::io::Read::read_to_string(raw.as_reader(), &mut body);
        let req = Request {
            method: raw.method().as_str().to_string(),
            path: raw.url().to_string(),
            headers: raw
                .headers()
                .iter()
                .map(|h| (h.field.as_str().as_str().to_string(), h.value.as_str().to_string()))
                .collect(),
            body,
        };
        let res = handle(&modes, &mut state, &req);
        let response = tiny_http::Response::from_string(res.body).with_status_code(res.status);
        let _ = raw.respond(response);
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

### Task 8: Transport, `transit fuzz`, and the parameter-defaulting bypass

**Files:**
- Create: `src/transport.rs`, `src/fuzz.rs`
- Modify: `src/corpus.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`, `tests/acceptance.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `hostile::{FailureModes, Ledger, Request, Response, handle}` from Task 7; `json::{parse, parse_strict, Config, Json}`.
- Produces: `transit::corpus::Evidence` (added here, beside `Condition`, because this is where the vocabulary first bites); `transit::transport::{Transport, InProcess, Http, RateLimiter, TransportError}` with `Transport::send(&mut self, &Request) -> Result<Response, TransportError>`, `Transport::describe(&self) -> String` and `Transport::evidence(&self) -> Evidence`; `transit::fuzz::{Finding, FuzzReport, policy_approves, parameter_defaulting_bypass, duplicate_key_split, alias_collapse, run_all}`. CLI: `transit fuzz [--target <url>] [--scenario <name>] [--format json]`.

One interface, two transports. `InProcess` calls `hostile::handle` directly — no socket, no port, no flake — and `Http` speaks to a real URL. The acceptance tests use `InProcess`, so they prove a property of the endpoint code rather than of a network round trip, and they never bind a port that a concurrently running session could collide with.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add `Evidence` to the shared vocabulary**

Append to the non-test portion of `src/corpus.rs`:

```rust
/// How a fact was come by. Attached to every verdict and every finding,
/// because the methodological crux of this whole paper is that a
/// documentation-derived classification and a measurement are not the same
/// kind of claim and must not print the same way.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Evidence {
    /// Read out of an OpenAPI document. A claim about the document.
    SpecDerived,
    /// Measured against the hostile endpoint in this repository.
    HostileServer,
    /// Measured against a real endpoint, under the authorization gate.
    LiveProbed,
    /// Taken from the desk study's documentation review. **A hypothesis.**
    DeskStudy,
}

impl Evidence {
    /// One line, printed next to every result. Nobody reading output should
    /// have to go and look this up.
    pub fn caveat(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Evidence::SpecDerived => {
                "derived from a specification; C1 and C4 are properties of an \
                 implementation and no document reveals them"
            }
            Evidence::HostileServer => {
                "measured against this repository's deliberately hostile endpoint"
            }
            Evidence::LiveProbed => "measured against a live endpoint",
            Evidence::DeskStudy => {
                "HYPOTHESIS from a documentation review, never measured; \
                 verifying it is work this paper has not done"
            }
        }
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {

    use crate::corpus::Evidence;
    use crate::hostile::FailureModes;
    use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

    #[test]
    fn the_in_process_transport_reaches_the_endpoint_and_keeps_its_state() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::all_hostile());
        let req = Request {
            method: "POST".into(),
            path: "/v1/transfer".into(),
            headers: vec![],
            body: r#"{"to":"acct-9","amount":100}"#.into(),
        };
        assert_eq!(t.send(&req).unwrap().status, 200);
        assert_eq!(t.send(&req).unwrap().status, 200);
        let state = t
            .send(&Request { method: "GET".into(), path: "/v1/state".into(), headers: vec![], body: String::new() })
            .unwrap();
        assert!(state.body.contains(r#""version":2"#), "{}", state.body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_in_process_transport_is_evidence_from_the_hostile_server() {
        assert_eq!(InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable()).evidence(), Evidence::HostileServer);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_rate_limiter_spaces_calls_without_this_test_sleeping() {
        // `next_delay` is pure so the policy can be tested at full speed;
        // `wait` is the thin wrapper that actually sleeps.
        let mut l = RateLimiter::per_minute(60); // one per second
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        assert_eq!(l.next_delay(t0), Duration::ZERO, "the first call waits for nothing");
        assert_eq!(l.next_delay(t0 + Duration::from_millis(200)), Duration::from_millis(800));
        assert_eq!(l.next_delay(t0 + Duration::from_secs(5)), Duration::ZERO);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unlimited_rate_never_waits() {
        let mut l = RateLimiter::unlimited();
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        assert_eq!(l.next_delay(t0), Duration::ZERO);
        assert_eq!(l.next_delay(t0), Duration::ZERO);
    }
}
```

Prepend to `src/transport.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::Evidence;
use crate::hostile::{handle, FailureModes, Ledger};
pub use crate::hostile::{Request, Response};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum TransportError {
    #[error("{url}: {message}")]
    Http { url: String, message: String },
}

/// One interface, two implementations: the endpoint in this process, and a
/// real one over HTTP. Everything above this line is written once.
pub trait Transport {
    fn send(&mut self, req: &Request) -> Result<Response, TransportError>;
    fn describe(&self) -> String;
    fn evidence(&self) -> Evidence;
}

/// The hostile endpoint, called directly. No socket, no port, no flake —
/// and the same `handle` the served version runs.
pub struct InProcess {
    modes: FailureModes,
    state: Ledger,
}

impl InProcess {
    pub fn new(modes: FailureModes) -> Self {
        InProcess { modes, state: Ledger::default() }
    }

    pub fn ledger(&self) -> &Ledger {
        &self.state
    }
}

impl Transport for InProcess {
    fn send(&mut self, req: &Request) -> Result<Response, TransportError> {
        Ok(handle(&self.modes, &mut self.state, req))
    }

    fn describe(&self) -> String {
        format!("in-process hostile endpoint {:?}", self.modes)
    }

    fn evidence(&self) -> Evidence {
        Evidence::HostileServer
    }
}

/// Spacing between calls. `next_delay` is pure so the policy is testable at
/// full speed; `wait` is the wrapper that sleeps.
pub struct RateLimiter {
    min_interval: Duration,
    last: Option<Instant>,
}

impl RateLimiter {
    pub fn per_minute(n: u32) -> Self {
        let min_interval = if n == 0 {
            Duration::from_secs(60)
        } else {
            Duration::from_secs_f64(60.0 / f64::from(n))
        };
        RateLimiter { min_interval, last: None }
    }

    pub fn unlimited() -> Self {
        RateLimiter { min_interval: Duration::ZERO, last: None }
    }

    pub fn next_delay(&mut self, now: Instant) -> Duration {
        let wait = match self.last {
            Some(prev) => self.min_interval.saturating_sub(now.duration_since(prev)),
            None => Duration::ZERO,
        };
        self.last = Some(now + wait);
        wait
    }

    pub fn wait(&mut self) {
        let d = self.next_delay(Instant::now());
        if !d.is_zero() {
            std::thread::sleep(d);
        }
    }
}

/// Whether a base URL names this machine.
///
/// Load-bearing rather than cosmetic: `transit fuzz --target` is **not**
/// gated, and `run_all` deliberately executes a non-idempotent operation
/// twice. It may therefore only ever point at a hostile endpoint the operator
/// is running locally. Anything else has to go through `probe` and its
/// authorization gate.
pub fn is_loopback(base: &str) -> bool {
    let authority = base
        .split_once("://")
        .map(|(_, rest)| rest)
        .unwrap_or(base)
        .split('/')
        .next()
        .unwrap_or("");
    // Strip any userinfo, then take the host out of `host:port`, handling the
    // bracketed IPv6 form before splitting on `:`.
    let hostport = authority.rsplit('@').next().unwrap_or("");
    let host = match hostport.strip_prefix('[') {
        Some(rest) => rest.split(']').next().unwrap_or(""),
        None => hostport.split(':').next().unwrap_or(""),
    };
    host == "localhost" || host == "::1" || host.starts_with("127.")
}

/// A real endpoint. Only ever constructed behind the authorization gate in
/// Task 11, or pointed at the locally served hostile endpoint.
pub struct Http {
    pub base: String,
    pub evidence: Evidence,
    pub limiter: RateLimiter,
}

impl Transport for Http {
    fn send(&mut self, req: &Request) -> Result<Response, TransportError> {
        self.limiter.wait();
        let url = format!("{}{}", self.base.trim_end_matches('/'), req.path);
        let mut r = match req.method.as_str() {
            "GET" => ureq::get(&url),
            "POST" => ureq::post(&url),
            other => {
                return Err(TransportError::Http {
                    url,
                    message: format!("unsupported method {other}"),
                })
            }
        };
        for (k, v) in &req.headers {
            r = r.set(k, v);
        }
        let sent = if req.method == "GET" {
            r.call()
        } else {
            r.set("Content-Type", "application/json").send_string(&req.body)
        };
        match sent {
            Ok(resp) => Ok(Response {
                status: resp.status(),
                body: resp.into_string().unwrap_or_default(),
            }),
            // ureq treats a non-2xx status as an error. For this tool a 409
            // or a 412 is the *answer*, so unwrap it back into a response.
            Err(ureq::Error::Status(code, resp)) => Ok(Response {
                status: code,
                body: resp.into_string().unwrap_or_default(),
            }),
            Err(e) => Err(TransportError::Http { url, message: e.to_string() }),
        }
    }

    fn describe(&self) -> String {
        self.base.clone()
    }

    fn evidence(&self) -> Evidence {
        self.evidence
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::hostile::FailureModes;
    use crate::transport::InProcess;

    #[test]
    fn the_policy_model_approves_a_standard_transfer_and_refuses_a_priority_one() {
        // The finding below is derived from this function, so if it were
        // wrong every finding would be too.
        assert!(policy_approves(&parse_strict(r#"{"to":"a","amount":1}"#).unwrap()));
        assert!(policy_approves(&parse_strict(r#"{"to":"a","amount":1,"fee_tier":"standard"}"#).unwrap()));
        assert!(!policy_approves(&parse_strict(r#"{"to":"a","amount":1,"fee_tier":"priority"}"#).unwrap()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_defaulting_bypass_is_found_against_a_defaulting_endpoint() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("default-injection").unwrap());
        let f = parameter_defaulting_bypass(&mut t).unwrap().expect("a finding");
        assert_eq!(f.condition, Condition::C1);
        assert!(f.endpoint_did.contains("priority"), "{}", f.endpoint_did);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_defaulting_bypass_disappears_against_a_boundable_endpoint() {
        // Without this, the check could be reporting a constant.
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        assert!(parameter_defaulting_bypass(&mut t).unwrap().is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_duplicate_key_split_is_found_only_when_the_endpoint_is_lenient() {
        let mut bad = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("duplicate-keys").unwrap());
        assert!(duplicate_key_split(&mut bad).unwrap().is_some());
        let mut good = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        assert!(duplicate_key_split(&mut good).unwrap().is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_alias_collapse_is_found_only_when_aliasing_is_on() {
        let mut bad = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("alias").unwrap());
        assert!(alias_collapse(&mut bad).unwrap().is_some());
        let mut good = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        assert!(alias_collapse(&mut good).unwrap().is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_report_carries_its_evidence_tag_and_lists_every_check_it_ran() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::all_hostile());
        let r = run_all(&mut t, "all-hostile").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(r.evidence, Evidence::HostileServer);
        assert!(r.checks_run.len() >= 3, "a check that did not run is not a pass");
        assert!(!r.findings.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_boundable_endpoint_yields_no_findings_but_still_reports_the_residual() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        let r = run_all(&mut t, "boundable").unwrap();
        assert!(r.findings.is_empty(), "{:?}", r.findings);
        assert!(r.checks_run.len() >= 6, "and every check still ran");
        assert!(r.checks_applicable);
        // The paper's position is that horn one of the retry ambiguity is
        // structural. Printing "clean" over it would contradict the argument
        // the tool exists to support.
        assert_eq!(r.residual.len(), 1, "the structural residual must survive");
        assert_eq!(r.residual[0].condition, Condition::C3);
    }
}
```
