- [ ] **Step 8: Write the fuzzer**

Prepend to `src/fuzz.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::{Condition, Evidence};
use crate::json::{parse_strict, Json};
use crate::transport::{Request, Response, Transport, TransportError};
use serde::Serialize;

/// One way an endpoint's effect escaped what the policy approved.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Finding {
    pub id: String,
    pub condition: Condition,
    pub title: String,
    /// What the policy engine evaluated and signed.
    pub policy_saw: String,
    /// What the endpoint actually did.
    pub endpoint_did: String,
    pub evidence: Evidence,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct FuzzReport {
    pub target: String,
    pub scenario: String,
    pub evidence: Evidence,
    /// Named so that a check which did not run cannot be mistaken for a
    /// check that found nothing.
    pub checks_run: Vec<String>,
    /// False when the target did not recognise the routes the checks are
    /// built around, so an empty `findings` means "not applicable" rather
    /// than "clean". Always true for the in-process endpoint.
    pub checks_applicable: bool,
    pub findings: Vec<Finding>,
    /// Findings that remain even against a fully conforming endpoint —
    /// structural consequences of the message layer that no endpoint change
    /// can close. Reported separately so a boundable run does not print
    /// "clean" over a gap the paper argues is permanent.
    pub residual: Vec<Finding>,
}

/// The policy the agent's authorization layer is modelling: ordinary
/// transfers are auto-approved, expedited ones are not. It reads the request
/// it was given and nothing else, which is exactly the assumption C1 breaks.
pub fn policy_approves(request: &Json) -> bool {
    match request.get("fee_tier").and_then(Json::as_string) {
        None => true,
        Some(t) => t == "standard",
    }
}

fn post(path: &str, body: &str, headers: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Request {
    Request {
        method: "POST".into(),
        path: path.into(),
        headers: headers.iter().map(|(a, b)| (a.to_string(), b.to_string())).collect(),
        body: body.into(),
    }
}

fn get(path: &str) -> Request {
    Request { method: "GET".into(), path: path.into(), headers: vec![], body: String::new() }
}

fn executed_field(res: &Response, field: &str) -> Option<String> {
    parse_strict(&res.body)
        .ok()?
        .get("executed")?
        .get(field)
        .and_then(Json::as_string)
}

/// C1. The agent omits `fee_tier`; the policy engine sees no fee tier, applies
/// the rule that auto-approves ordinary transfers, and signs. The endpoint
/// fills the gap from profile state the signature never covered, and performs
/// an action the policy would have refused.
pub fn parameter_defaulting_bypass<T: Transport>(
    t: &mut T,
) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let signed = r#"{"to":"acct-9","amount":100}"#;
    let Ok(parsed) = parse_strict(signed) else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    if !policy_approves(&parsed) {
        // The premise of the attack is that the policy said yes. If it did
        // not, there is nothing to report.
        return Ok(None);
    }

    // A well-behaved client sends the preconditions the endpoint asks for.
    // Omitting `If-Match` would get a 428 from every scenario that leaves
    // state pinning on — including `default-injection`, which turns on only
    // the defaulting — and the check would silently find nothing. The bypass
    // being demonstrated is about C1 alone, so C3 and C4 are satisfied here.
    let res = t.send(&post(
        "/v1/transfer",
        signed,
        &[("Idempotency-Key", "fuzz-c1"), ("If-Match", "0")],
    ))?;
    if res.status != 200 {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    let Some(fee_tier) = executed_field(&res, "fee_tier") else {
        return Ok(None);
    };

    // Re-run the policy over what the endpoint actually did. The finding is
    // derived from that comparison rather than asserted.
    let executed = format!(r#"{{"to":"acct-9","amount":100,"fee_tier":"{fee_tier}"}}"#);
    let Ok(executed_parsed) = parse_strict(&executed) else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    if policy_approves(&executed_parsed) {
        return Ok(None);
    }

    Ok(Some(Finding {
        id: "c1-parameter-defaulting".into(),
        condition: Condition::C1,
        title: "the endpoint executed an action the policy would have refused".into(),
        policy_saw: format!("{signed} — no fee_tier, so the ordinary-transfer rule approved it"),
        endpoint_did: format!("fee_tier resolved to `{fee_tier}` from server-side profile state"),
        evidence: t.evidence(),
    }))
}

/// C2. One wire form, duplicate `to` keys. A first-key-wins policy engine
/// authorizes the first recipient; the endpoint pays whoever its own decoder
/// picked.
pub fn duplicate_key_split<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let wire = r#"{"to":"acct-1","amount":100,"fee_tier":"standard","to":"acct-9"}"#;
    let res = t.send(&post("/v1/transfer", wire, &[("Idempotency-Key", "fuzz-c2"), ("If-Match", "0")]))?;
    if res.status != 200 {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    let Some(to) = executed_field(&res, "to") else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    if to == "acct-1" {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    Ok(Some(Finding {
        id: "c2-duplicate-key".into(),
        condition: Condition::C2,
        title: "the endpoint resolved a duplicate key differently from the policy engine".into(),
        policy_saw: "to = acct-1, the first binding a first-key-wins decoder returns".into(),
        endpoint_did: format!("paid `{to}`"),
        evidence: t.evidence(),
    }))
}

/// C2. Two spellings of one resource: an alias and the identifier it resolves
/// to. Distinct canonical bytes, identical effect.
pub fn alias_collapse<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let via_alias = t.send(&get("/v1/users/me"))?;
    if via_alias.status != 200 {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    let direct = t.send(&get("/v1/users/usr_99812"))?;
    if direct.status != 200 {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    let same = parse_strict(&via_alias.body)
        .ok()
        .and_then(|v| v.get("id").and_then(Json::as_string))
        == parse_strict(&direct.body)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.get("id").and_then(Json::as_string));
    if !same {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    Ok(Some(Finding {
        id: "c2-uri-alias".into(),
        condition: Condition::C2,
        title: "two canonical forms name one resource, so canonicalization is not injective".into(),
        policy_saw: "/v1/users/me".into(),
        endpoint_did: "resolved to usr_99812, the same resource /v1/users/usr_99812 names".into(),
        evidence: t.evidence(),
    }))
}

/// One adversarial check. Named rather than written inline as
/// `fn(&mut T) -> _`: an inferred return type in a cast position resolves
/// only while `TransportError` has exactly one applicable `From` impl, so the
/// day someone adds a `#[from]` variant the array stops compiling with
/// "type annotations needed" a long way from the cause.
type Check<T> = fn(&mut T) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError>;

/// Post every corpus payload to the endpoint and report where what it
/// executed disagrees with what a strict decoder reads from the same bytes.
///
/// The corpus exists to be *delivered*, not only compared between decoders.
/// Without this, lone surrogates, NFC/NFD drift, negative zero, underflow and
/// the big-integer case never reach an endpoint at all, and the design's
/// `fuzz` component is only half-built.
pub fn corpus_against_endpoint<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Vec<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (i, case) in crate::corpus::cases().into_iter().enumerate() {
        let _ = t.send(&post("/v1/reset", "{}", &[]));
        // Each payload carries a recipient and an amount so the endpoint has
        // something to execute; the corpus case supplies the hostile shape.
        let body = case.payload.replacen('{', r#"{"amount":1,"fee_tier":"standard","#, 1);
        let res = t.send(&post(
            "/v1/transfer",
            &body,
            &[("Idempotency-Key", &format!("corpus-{i}")), ("If-Match", "0")],
        ))?;
        if res.status != 200 {
            // A refusal is the correct behaviour, and is not a finding.
            continue;
        }
        let Some(executed) = executed_field(&res, "to") else {
            continue;
        };
        // The endpoint executed. What would a strict decoder have read?
        let strict = crate::json::parse_strict(&body)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.get("to").and_then(Json::as_string));
        if strict.as_deref() == Some(executed.as_str()) {
            continue;
        }
        out.push(Finding {
            id: format!("c2-corpus-{}", case.name),
            condition: case.condition,
            title: format!("`{}` executed against a payload strict ingestion refuses", case.name),
            policy_saw: match strict {
                Some(s) => format!("a strict decoder reads `{s}`"),
                None => "a strict decoder refuses these bytes outright".to_string(),
            },
            endpoint_did: format!("executed a transfer to `{executed}`"),
            evidence: t.evidence(),
        });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

pub fn run_all<T: Transport>(t: &mut T, scenario: &str) -> Result<FuzzReport, TransportError> {
    let target = t.describe();
    let evidence = t.evidence();
    let mut findings = Vec::new();
    let mut residual = Vec::new();
    let mut checks_run = Vec::new();

    let checks: [(&str, Check<T>); 4] = [
        ("c1-parameter-defaulting", parameter_defaulting_bypass),
        ("c2-duplicate-key", duplicate_key_split),
        ("c2-uri-alias", alias_collapse),
        ("c3-retry-ambiguity", retry_finding),
    ];
    for (name, f) in checks {
        // Reset between checks so one check's state cannot mask another's.
        let _ = t.send(&post("/v1/reset", "{}", &[]));
        checks_run.push(name.to_string());
        if let Some(finding) = f(t)? {
            findings.push(finding);
        }
    }

    let _ = t.send(&post("/v1/reset", "{}", &[]));
    checks_run.push("c2-corpus".to_string());
    findings.extend(corpus_against_endpoint(t)?);

    // The structural residual, reported separately. Horn one of the retry
    // ambiguity survives every endpoint fix, so a conforming endpoint must
    // not print "clean" over it.
    let _ = t.send(&post("/v1/reset", "{}", &[]));
    checks_run.push("c3-retry-residual".to_string());
    if let Some(f) = retry_residual(t)? {
        residual.push(f);
    }

    Ok(FuzzReport {
        target,
        scenario: scenario.to_string(),
        evidence,
        checks_run,
        // The in-process endpoint always recognises its own routes; `probe`
        // overwrites this after asking a real target.
        checks_applicable: true,
        findings,
        residual,
    })
}
```

```rust
use transit::corpus::{Condition, Evidence};
use transit::fuzz;
use transit::hostile::FailureModes;
use transit::transport::InProcess;

/// Acceptance test 2: `fuzz` against the hostile server in its
/// default-injection scenario reproduces the parameter-defaulting policy
/// bypass — a payload the policy layer approves and the endpoint executes
/// differently.
#[test]
fn fuzz_reproduces_the_parameter_defaulting_policy_bypass() {
    let mut hostile = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("default-injection").unwrap());
    let f = fuzz::parameter_defaulting_bypass(&mut hostile)
        .unwrap()
        .expect("the bypass must reproduce");

    assert_eq!(f.condition, Condition::C1);
    assert_eq!(f.evidence, Evidence::HostileServer);
    assert!(f.policy_saw.contains("no fee_tier"), "{}", f.policy_saw);
    assert!(f.endpoint_did.contains("priority"), "{}", f.endpoint_did);

    // The same request against an endpoint conforming to the Boundable
    // Profile produces nothing. Without this half, the check could be
    // reporting a constant and would still pass.
    let mut boundable = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
    assert!(
        fuzz::parameter_defaulting_bypass(&mut boundable).unwrap().is_none(),
        "the finding must be a property of the endpoint, not of the checker"
    );
}
```

```rust
    /// Run the adversarial checks against a target. Defaults to the hostile
    /// endpoint in this process, which needs no server and no credentials.
    Fuzz {
        /// A base URL. Omit to use the in-process hostile endpoint.
        #[arg(long)]
        target: Option<String>,
        #[arg(long, default_value = "all-hostile")]
        scenario: String,
        #[arg(long, default_value = "text")]
        format: Format,
    },
```

And to the `match`:

```rust
        Cmd::Fuzz { target, scenario, format } => {
            let modes = transit::hostile::FailureModes::named(&scenario)?;
            let report = match target {
                None => {
                    let mut t = transit::transport::InProcess::new(modes);
                    transit::fuzz::run_all(&mut t, &scenario)?
                }
                Some(base) => {
                    // `fuzz` has no authorization gate, and `run_all`
                    // deliberately executes a non-idempotent operation twice.
                    // Pointing it at a stranger would be both ungated and
                    // mislabelled, since its findings are tagged
                    // `HostileServer`. Refuse anything but loopback.
                    if !transit::transport::is_loopback(&base) {
                        eprintln!(
                            "refusing: `fuzz --target` is ungated and sends adversarial \
                             traffic, including a deliberate double execution, so it may \
                             only point at a hostile endpoint you are running locally. \
                             `{base}` is not loopback.\n\nTo test something real, use \
                             `transit probe`, which requires a written authorization \
                             declaration, a contact address, and --i-am-authorized."
                        );
                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(2));
                    }
                    let mut t = transit::transport::Http {
                        base,
                        evidence: transit::corpus::Evidence::HostileServer,
                        limiter: transit::transport::RateLimiter::per_minute(600),
                    };
                    transit::fuzz::run_all(&mut t, &scenario)?
                }
            };
            if format == Format::Json {
                println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&report)?);
            } else {
                println!("target:   {}", report.target);
                println!("evidence: {:?} — {}", report.evidence, report.evidence.caveat());
                println!("checks:   {}", report.checks_run.join(", "));
                for f in &report.findings {
                    println!("\n{} [{}]", f.title, f.condition.title());
                    println!("  policy saw:   {}", f.policy_saw);
                    println!("  endpoint did: {}", f.endpoint_did);
                }
                if report.findings.is_empty() {
                    println!("\nno findings");
                }
            }
            Ok(if report.findings.is_empty() {
                ExitCode::SUCCESS
            } else {
                ExitCode::from(1)
            })
        }
```

Note the `--target` path tags its evidence `HostileServer`, not `LiveProbed`. Probing a real endpoint goes through `transit probe` and its gate, in Task 11; `fuzz --target` exists for driving a *locally served* hostile endpoint, and the README says so.

### Task 9: The retry ambiguity

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/fuzz.rs`, `tests/acceptance.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Transport`, `hostile::handle` semantics from Tasks 7–8.
- Produces: `transit::fuzz::{RetryBranch, ClientBelief, RetryOutcome, retry_ambiguity}`. `retry_ambiguity<T: Transport>(&mut T) -> Result<Vec<RetryOutcome>, TransportError>` returns exactly two outcomes: the same-nonce branch and the fresh-nonce branch. `run_all` gains a fourth check that turns a both-branches-wrong result into a C3 finding.

The dropped response is simulated on the client side — the driver sends the request and discards the answer, which is precisely what a TCP reset does to a client. Nothing is faked at the endpoint; its state really did change.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Append to the test module in `src/fuzz.rs`:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn the_same_nonce_branch_leaves_the_client_believing_a_committed_write_failed() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("retry-ambiguity").unwrap());
        let outcomes = retry_ambiguity(&mut t).unwrap();
        let a = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::SameNonce).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(a.client_believes, ClientBelief::Failed);
        assert_eq!(a.orders_committed, 1, "the state change happened anyway");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_fresh_nonce_branch_executes_the_operation_twice() {
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("retry-ambiguity").unwrap());
        let outcomes = retry_ambiguity(&mut t).unwrap();
        let b = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::FreshNonce).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(b.client_believes, ClientBelief::Succeeded);
        assert_eq!(b.orders_committed, 2, "one order too many");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_boundable_endpoint_makes_the_fresh_nonce_branch_correct() {
        // The point of the paper's C3: decoupling the business idempotency
        // key from the authorization nonce is what removes the second horn.
        let mut t = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        let outcomes = retry_ambiguity(&mut t).unwrap();
        let b = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::FreshNonce).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(b.orders_committed, 1, "a retry must not duplicate the effect");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_retry_check_produces_a_c3_finding_only_when_both_branches_are_wrong() {
        let mut bad = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("retry-ambiguity").unwrap());
        let f = retry_finding(&mut bad).unwrap().expect("a finding");
        assert_eq!(f.condition, Condition::C3);
        assert!(f.endpoint_did.contains("twice"), "{}", f.endpoint_did);

        let mut good = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
        assert!(retry_finding(&mut good).unwrap().is_none());
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Append to the non-test portion of `src/fuzz.rs`:

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub enum RetryBranch {
    /// The proxy retries with the capability it already has.
    SameNonce,
    /// The proxy obtains a fresh signature over a new nonce.
    FreshNonce,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub enum ClientBelief {
    Succeeded,
    Failed,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct RetryOutcome {
    pub branch: RetryBranch,
    pub client_believes: ClientBelief,
    pub orders_committed: usize,
    pub retry_status: u16,
}

fn order_count<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<usize, TransportError> {
    let res = t.send(&get("/v1/state"))?;
    Ok(parse_strict(&res.body)
        .ok()
        .and_then(|v| match v.get("orders") {
            Some(Json::Array(a)) => Some(a.len()),
            _ => None,
        })
        .unwrap_or(0))
}

/// The retry ambiguity, run out to both of its ends.
///
/// A non-idempotent POST commits and returns 200. A TCP reset drops the
/// response — modelled here by the client discarding it, which is exactly
/// what the client experiences. Then either the proxy retries with the same
/// capability, and the nonce cache calls it a replay so the client believes
/// the operation failed although the state changed; or the proxy obtains a
/// fresh signature, and the operation executes a second time. Neither branch
/// is right, and no message-level mechanism can tell an adversarial replay
/// from an infrastructure retry, because that distinction does not exist at
/// the message layer.
pub fn retry_ambiguity<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Vec<RetryOutcome>, TransportError> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();

    for branch in [RetryBranch::SameNonce, RetryBranch::FreshNonce] {
        t.send(&post("/v1/reset", "{}", &[]))?;

        // The first call commits. Its response is dropped on the floor.
        let first = post("/v1/orders", "{}", &[("Authorization", "Capability nonce=n1")]);
        let _discarded = t.send(&first)?;

        let retry_nonce = match branch {
            RetryBranch::SameNonce => "n1",
            RetryBranch::FreshNonce => "n2",
        };
        let retry = post(
            "/v1/orders",
            "{}",
            &[("Authorization", &format!("Capability nonce={retry_nonce}"))],
        );
        let res = t.send(&retry)?;

        out.push(RetryOutcome {
            branch,
            client_believes: if (200..300).contains(&res.status) {
                ClientBelief::Succeeded
            } else {
                ClientBelief::Failed
            },
            orders_committed: order_count(t)?,
            retry_status: res.status,
        });
    }

    Ok(out)
}

/// A C3 finding, raised only when *both* branches are wrong: one where the
/// client is told the operation failed although state changed, and one where
/// it executes twice.
pub fn retry_finding<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let outcomes = retry_ambiguity(t)?;
    let same = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::SameNonce);
    let fresh = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::FreshNonce);
    let (Some(same), Some(fresh)) = (same, fresh) else {
        return Ok(None);
    };

    let believed_failed_but_committed =
        same.client_believes == ClientBelief::Failed && same.orders_committed >= 1;
    let executed_twice = fresh.orders_committed >= 2;
    if !(believed_failed_but_committed && executed_twice) {
        return Ok(None);
    }

    Ok(Some(Finding {
        id: "c3-retry-ambiguity".into(),
        condition: Condition::C3,
        title: "neither branch of a network retry is correct".into(),
        policy_saw: format!(
            "one authorized order; the retry with the same nonce returned {} so the client \
             recorded a failure",
            same.retry_status
        ),
        endpoint_did: format!(
            "committed {} order despite the reported failure, and with a fresh nonce executed \
             twice ({} orders)",
            same.orders_committed, fresh.orders_committed
        ),
        evidence: t.evidence(),
    }))
}
```

And the structural half, which no endpoint fix closes:

```rust
/// Horn one on its own: the client is told the operation failed while the
/// state change stands. This survives **every** endpoint fix, because the
/// message layer cannot distinguish an adversarial replay from an
/// infrastructure retry — the distinction does not exist there. It is
/// reported as `residual` rather than as a finding, so a conforming endpoint
/// does not print "no findings" over a gap the paper argues is permanent.
pub fn retry_residual<T: Transport>(t: &mut T) -> Result<Option<Finding>, TransportError> {
    let outcomes = retry_ambiguity(t)?;
    let Some(same) = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::SameNonce) else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    if !(same.client_believes == ClientBelief::Failed && same.orders_committed >= 1) {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    Ok(Some(Finding {
        id: "c3-retry-residual".into(),
        condition: Condition::C3,
        title: "a committed operation was reported to the client as failed".into(),
        policy_saw: format!("the retry was refused with {}", same.retry_status),
        endpoint_did: format!(
            "kept the {} order it had already committed; no message-level mechanism can tell \
             this retry from a replay",
            same.orders_committed
        ),
        evidence: t.evidence(),
    }))
}
```

`run_all` in Task 8 already lists `retry_finding` as its fourth check and
calls `retry_residual` afterwards, so no change is needed there.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write acceptance test 3**

Append to `tests/acceptance.rs`:

```rust
use transit::fuzz::{ClientBelief, RetryBranch};

/// Acceptance test 3: `fuzz` against the non-idempotent scenario reproduces
/// the retry ambiguity — one branch where the client believes the operation
/// failed although state changed, one where it executes twice.
#[test]
fn fuzz_reproduces_both_horns_of_the_retry_ambiguity() {
    let mut hostile = InProcess::new(FailureModes::named("retry-ambiguity").unwrap());
    let outcomes = fuzz::retry_ambiguity(&mut hostile).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(outcomes.len(), 2);

    let same = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::SameNonce).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(same.client_believes, ClientBelief::Failed);
    assert_eq!(same.orders_committed, 1, "the state change happened regardless");

    let fresh = outcomes.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::FreshNonce).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(fresh.client_believes, ClientBelief::Succeeded);
    assert_eq!(fresh.orders_committed, 2, "the effect happened twice");

    // Decoupling the business idempotency key from the authorization nonce
    // is what closes the second horn. Asserting that here is what keeps this
    // test a statement about C3 rather than about the mock.
    let mut boundable = InProcess::new(FailureModes::boundable());
    let fixed = fuzz::retry_ambiguity(&mut boundable).unwrap();
    let fresh = fixed.iter().find(|o| o.branch == RetryBranch::FreshNonce).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(fresh.orders_committed, 1);
}
```

```bash
The dropped response is modelled on the client side, by discarding it,
```

### Task 10: `transit classify`, and acceptance test 4

**Files:**
- Create: `src/classify.rs`, `examples/iso20022-pain001.openapi.json`, `examples/s3-put-if-match.openapi.json`, `examples/stripe-charges.openapi.json`, `examples/slack-chat-postmessage.openapi.json`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`, `tests/acceptance.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `corpus::{Condition, Evidence}`.
- Produces: `transit::classify::{Verdict, ConditionVerdict, Classification, classify_spec, ClassifyError}`. `classify_spec(spec_json: &str) -> Result<Vec<Classification>, ClassifyError>` returns one `Classification` per operation. CLI: `transit classify --spec <openapi.json> [--format json]`, exit 1 when any operation is `NonQualifying`.

**`Unknown` is a first-class verdict, and that is the point.** C1 and C4 are properties of an implementation; an OpenAPI document does not reveal server-side defaulting and cannot say whether an effect depends on server state. Treating a missing signal as a *failure* would let the tool assert things a document cannot support. Treating it as `Unknown` — which caps the result at `Conditional` — is the honest reading, and it is why every classification carries `Evidence::SpecDerived`.

Expect the classifier to disagree with the desk study on some rows. **Report the disagreement; do not tune the rules until they match.** A classifier fitted to reproduce a documentation review has stopped being evidence about anything.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn one(spec: &str) -> Classification {
        classify_spec(spec).unwrap().into_iter().next().unwrap()
    }

    fn spec_with(op: &str) -> String {
        format!(r#"{{"openapi":"3.0.3","info":{{"title":"t","version":"1"}},"paths":{{"/x":{{{op}}}}}}}"#)
    }

    const FULLY_PINNED: &str = r#""post":{
      "operationId":"op",
      "parameters":[
        {"name":"Idempotency-Key","in":"header","required":true,"schema":{"type":"string"}},
        {"name":"If-Match","in":"header","required":true,"schema":{"type":"string"}}],
      "requestBody":{"required":true,"content":{"application/json":{"schema":{
        "type":"object","additionalProperties":false,
        "required":["a"],"properties":{"a":{"type":"string"}}}}}}}"#;

    #[test]
    fn a_fully_pinned_operation_qualifies() {
        let c = one(&spec_with(FULLY_PINNED));
        assert_eq!(c.verdict, Verdict::Qualified);
        assert!(c.failing.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(c.evidence, Evidence::SpecDerived);

    #[test]

        let c = one(&spec_with(&op));
        assert_eq!(c.verdict, Verdict::Conditional);
        assert!(c.failing.is_empty(), "the client can still volunteer one");

            "",

        let op = FULLY_PINNED.replace(

        let spec = r#"{"openapi":"3.0.3","info":{"title":"t","version":"1"},
          "paths":{"/x":{"post":{"operationId":"op",
            "parameters":[
              {"name":"Idempotency-Key","in":"header","required":true,"schema":{"type":"string"}},
              {"name":"If-Match","in":"header","required":true,"schema":{"type":"string"}}],
            "requestBody":{"required":true,"content":{"application/json":{
              "schema":{"$ref":"#/components/schemas/Body"}}}}}}},
          "components":{"schemas":{"Body":{"type":"object","additionalProperties":false,
            "required":["a"],"properties":{"a":{"type":"string"}}}}}}"#;
        assert_eq!(one(spec).verdict, Verdict::Qualified);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_self_referential_ref_terminates() {
        let spec = r#"{"openapi":"3.0.3","info":{"title":"t","version":"1"},
          "paths":{"/x":{"post":{"operationId":"op",
            "requestBody":{"content":{"application/json":{
              "schema":{"$ref":"#/components/schemas/Node"}}}}}}},
          "components":{"schemas":{"Node":{"type":"object",
            "properties":{"child":{"$ref":"#/components/schemas/Node"}}}}}}"#;
        assert!(classify_spec(spec).is_ok(), "a legal recursive schema must not hang");
    }

    #[test]
    fn garbage_is_an_error_not_a_panic() {
        for bad in [
            "", "{", "null", "[]", r#"{"paths":5}"#, r#"{"openapi":"3.0.3"}"#,
            // Swagger 2.0 is refused explicitly rather than misread as an
            // OpenAPI 3 document with no operations.
            r#"{"swagger":"2.0","paths":{}}"#,
            r#"{"paths":{"/x":{"post":{"parameters":"not an array"}}}}"#,
            r#"{"paths":{"/x":{"post":{"requestBody":{"content":{"application/json":{"schema":5}}}}}}}"#,
        ] {
            assert!(classify_spec(bad).is_err(), "expected an error for {bad:?}");
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/classify.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::{Condition, Evidence};
use serde::Serialize;
use serde_json::Value;

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ClassifyError {
    #[error("could not parse the specification: {0}")]
    Parse(#[from] serde_json::Error),
    #[error("this does not look like an OpenAPI document: {why}")]
    NotOpenApi { why: String },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub enum Verdict {
    /// C1–C4 all satisfied as specified.
    Qualified,
    /// Nothing failed, but something is unknown or depends on the client
    /// volunteering more than the specification requires.
    Conditional,
    /// At least one condition cannot be met at all.
    NonQualifying,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize)]
#[serde(tag = "state", rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum ConditionVerdict {
    Satisfied,
    /// A specification cannot answer this question. Caps the result at
    /// `Conditional` rather than asserting a failure the document does not
    /// support.
    Unknown { why: String },
    /// Achievable, but only if the client sends more than it is obliged to.
    ClientDependent { how: String },
    /// There is nothing a well-behaved client could do about this.
    Failed { why: String },
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Classification {
    pub operation: String,
    pub method: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub conditions: Vec<(Condition, ConditionVerdict)>,
    pub verdict: Verdict,
    /// The specific conditions that failed, named so a reader knows which
    /// one is load-bearing for a given deployment.
    pub failing: Vec<Condition>,
    pub evidence: Evidence,
}

impl Classification {
    pub fn verdict_for(&self, c: Condition) -> Option<&ConditionVerdict> {
        self.conditions.iter().find(|(k, _)| *k == c).map(|(_, v)| v)
    }
}

/// Phrases that betray server-side defaulting in prose. Crude on purpose:
/// this is a documentation heuristic and is labelled as one. It fires on
/// evidence *for* a failure and never on its absence.
const SERVER_DEFAULT_PHRASES: &[&str] = &[
    "user profile",
    "account default",
    "workspace default",
    "inherited from",
    "server-side default",
    "derived from the account",
];

const ALIAS_PHRASES: &[&str] = &[
    "id or name",
    "name or id",
    "channel name",
    "or alias",
    "alias for",
    "may be an alias",
];

const IDEMPOTENCY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
    "idempotency-key",
    "idempotency_key",
    "x-idempotency-key",
    "idempotencytoken",
    "client-token",
    "clienttoken",
    "request-id",
    "request_id",
    "requestid",
];

const PINNING_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
    "if-match",
    "if-unmodified-since",
    "etag",
    "version",
    "resource_version",
    "cas",
    "sha",
    "parent_sha",
    "base_commit",
];

/// Methods RFC 9110 defines as idempotent.
const IDEMPOTENT_METHODS: &[&str] = &["GET", "HEAD", "PUT", "DELETE", "OPTIONS", "TRACE"];

/// Maximum `$ref` hops. Bounded rather than cycle-detected: a self-referential
/// schema is legal OpenAPI, and this classifier only needs the top two levels
/// of any schema, so stopping is both safe and sufficient.
const MAX_REF_DEPTH: usize = 16;

/// Inline local `$ref`s before classifying.
///
/// Not optional polish: **every** real OpenAPI document — the actual ISO
/// 20022, S3 and Stripe specifications included — puts its schemas behind
/// `$ref`. A classifier that does not resolve them sees an object whose only
/// key is `$ref`, finds no properties, and returns `Unknown` for C1 and C2 on
/// every operation, collapsing silently to `Conditional`. The four examples
/// shipped here are hand-inlined, so without this the gap would be invisible
/// in testing and universal in practice.
///
/// Remote and file `$ref`s are deliberately not followed — resolving those
/// would mean fetching, and `classify` touches no network.
fn inline_refs(doc: &Value, node: &Value, depth: usize) -> Value {
    if depth >= MAX_REF_DEPTH {
        return node.clone();
    }
    match node {
        Value::Object(m) => {
            if let Some(Value::String(r)) = m.get("$ref") {
                let Some(rest) = r.strip_prefix("#/") else {
                    // A remote or file reference. Left as-is, and the verdict
                    // it produces is `Unknown` rather than a guess.
                    return node.clone();
                };
                let mut cur = doc;
                for seg in rest.split('/') {
                    // RFC 6901 escaping, the only two that matter here.
                    let seg = seg.replace("~1", "/").replace("~0", "~");
                    match cur.get(&seg) {
                        Some(next) => cur = next,
                        None => return node.clone(),
                    }
                }
                return inline_refs(doc, &cur.clone(), depth + 1);
            }
            Value::Object(
                m.iter()
                    .map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), inline_refs(doc, v, depth + 1)))
                    .collect(),
            )
        }
        Value::Array(a) => Value::Array(a.iter().map(|v| inline_refs(doc, v, depth + 1)).collect()),
        other => other.clone(),
    }
}

pub fn classify_spec(spec_json: &str) -> Result<Vec<Classification>, ClassifyError> {
    let raw: Value = serde_json::from_str(spec_json)?;
    if raw.get("swagger").is_some() && raw.get("openapi").is_none() {
        return Err(ClassifyError::NotOpenApi {
            why: "this is a Swagger 2.0 document; only OpenAPI 3.x is understood".into(),
        });
    }
    let doc = inline_refs(&raw, &raw, 0);
    let paths = doc.get("paths").and_then(Value::as_object).ok_or_else(|| {
        ClassifyError::NotOpenApi { why: "no `paths` object".into() }
    })?;

    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (path, item) in paths {
        let Some(item) = item.as_object() else { continue };
        for method in ["get", "post", "put", "patch", "delete"] {
            let Some(op) = item.get(method).and_then(Value::as_object) else {
                continue;
            };
            out.push(classify_operation(path, method, op, item));
        }
    }
    if out.is_empty() {
        return Err(ClassifyError::NotOpenApi { why: "no operations found".into() });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

struct Param<'a> {
    name: String,
    /// `path`, `query`, `header` or `cookie`. Load-bearing for C1: an
    /// optional *query* parameter is un-signed server-side input, while a
    /// path parameter is structurally required by the template.
    location: String,
    required: bool,
    description: String,
    schema: Option<&'a Value>,
}

fn parameters<'a>(
    op: &'a serde_json::Map<String, Value>,
    item: &'a serde_json::Map<String, Value>,
) -> Vec<Param<'a>> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for src in [item.get("parameters"), op.get("parameters")].into_iter().flatten() {
        let Some(list) = src.as_array() else { continue };
        for p in list {
            let Some(name) = p.get("name").and_then(Value::as_str) else {
                continue;
            };
            out.push(Param {
                name: name.to_ascii_lowercase(),
                location: p
                    .get("in")
                    .and_then(Value::as_str)
                    .unwrap_or("query")
                    .to_ascii_lowercase(),
                required: p.get("required").and_then(Value::as_bool).unwrap_or(false),
                description: p
                    .get("description")
                    .and_then(Value::as_str)
                    .unwrap_or("")
                    .to_ascii_lowercase(),
                schema: p.get("schema"),
            });
        }
    }
    out
}

fn body_schema(op: &serde_json::Map<String, Value>) -> Option<&Value> {
    op.get("requestBody")?
        .get("content")?
        .get("application/json")?
        .get("schema")
}

fn contains_any(haystack: &str, needles: &[&str]) -> bool {
    needles.iter().any(|n| haystack.contains(n))
}

fn classify_operation(
    path: &str,
    method: &str,
    op: &serde_json::Map<String, Value>,
    item: &serde_json::Map<String, Value>,
) -> Classification {
    let params = parameters(op, item);
    let schema = body_schema(op);
    let method_upper = method.to_ascii_uppercase();

    let conditions = vec![
        (Condition::C1, judge_c1(schema, &params)),
        (Condition::C2, judge_c2(path, schema, &params)),
        (Condition::C3, judge_c3(&method_upper, schema, &params)),
        (Condition::C4, judge_c4(schema, &params)),
    ];

    let failing: Vec<Condition> = conditions
        .iter()
        .filter(|(_, v)| matches!(v, ConditionVerdict::Failed { .. }))
        .map(|(c, _)| *c)
        .collect();

    let verdict = if !failing.is_empty() {
        Verdict::NonQualifying
    } else if conditions.iter().all(|(_, v)| matches!(v, ConditionVerdict::Satisfied)) {
        Verdict::Qualified
    } else {
        Verdict::Conditional
    };

    Classification {
        operation: op
            .get("operationId")
            .and_then(Value::as_str)
            .unwrap_or("(unnamed)")
            .to_string(),
        method: method_upper,
        path: path.to_string(),
        conditions,
        verdict,
        failing,
        evidence: Evidence::SpecDerived,
    }
}

/// Every property of the body schema, as `(name, schema)`.
fn properties(schema: Option<&Value>) -> Vec<(String, &Value)> {
    let Some(props) = schema.and_then(|s| s.get("properties")).and_then(Value::as_object) else {
        return Vec::new();
    };
    props.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.to_ascii_lowercase(), v)).collect()
}

fn required_names(schema: Option<&Value>) -> Vec<String> {
    schema
        .and_then(|s| s.get("required"))
        .and_then(Value::as_array)
        .map(|a| {
            a.iter()
                .filter_map(Value::as_str)
                .map(str::to_ascii_lowercase)
                .collect()
        })
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

fn description_of(v: &Value) -> String {
    v.get("description")
        .and_then(Value::as_str)
        .unwrap_or("")
        .to_ascii_lowercase()
}

fn judge_c1(schema: Option<&Value>, params: &[Param]) -> ConditionVerdict {
    // A documented server-side default is a positive signal of failure: the
    // client cannot override state it never sees.
    for (name, prop) in properties(schema) {
        if prop.get("x-transit-server-default").and_then(Value::as_bool) == Some(true)
            || contains_any(&description_of(prop), SERVER_DEFAULT_PHRASES)
        {
            return ConditionVerdict::Failed {
                why: format!("`{name}` is documented as filled from server-side state"),
            };
        }
    }
    for p in params {
        if contains_any(&p.description, SERVER_DEFAULT_PHRASES) {
            return ConditionVerdict::Failed {
                why: format!("`{}` is documented as filled from server-side state", p.name),
            };
        }
    }

    let Some(schema) = schema else {
        return ConditionVerdict::Unknown {
            why: "no JSON request body is described, so the parameter space is unknown".into(),
        };
    };
    let props = properties(Some(schema));
    if props.is_empty() {
        return ConditionVerdict::Unknown {
            why: "the request body schema declares no properties".into(),
        };
    }
    let required = required_names(Some(schema));
    let closed = schema.get("additionalProperties").and_then(Value::as_bool) == Some(false);

    let mut volunteer: Vec<String> = props
        .iter()
        .filter(|(n, p)| !required.contains(n) || p.get("default").is_some())
        .map(|(n, _)| n.clone())
        .collect();
    // Optional parameters outside the body count too. P02's worked example is
    // `POST /v1/transfer?to=acct-9&fee_tier=priority` — a *query* parameter —
    // so a rule that reads only the request body would return `Satisfied` on
    // the paper's own headline case. Path parameters are excluded: they are
    // structurally required by the template.
    for p in params.iter().filter(|p| !p.required && p.location != "path") {
        volunteer.push(format!("{} ({})", p.name, p.location));
    }
    if !closed {
        volunteer.push("(additionalProperties is not `false`)".into());
    }
    if volunteer.is_empty() {
        ConditionVerdict::Satisfied
    } else {
        volunteer.sort();
        ConditionVerdict::ClientDependent {
            how: format!("send every optional parameter explicitly: {}", volunteer.join(", ")),
        }
    }
}

fn judge_c2(path: &str, schema: Option<&Value>, params: &[Param]) -> ConditionVerdict {
    for seg in path.split('/') {
        if seg.eq_ignore_ascii_case("me") || seg.eq_ignore_ascii_case("self") {
            return ConditionVerdict::Failed {
                why: format!("`{seg}` is an alias for a concrete identifier, so two canonical forms name one resource"),
            };
        }
    }
    for (name, prop) in properties(schema) {
        if prop.get("x-transit-alias").and_then(Value::as_bool) == Some(true)
            || contains_any(&description_of(prop), ALIAS_PHRASES)
        {
            return ConditionVerdict::Failed {
                why: format!("`{name}` accepts more than one spelling of the same resource"),
            };
        }
    }
    for p in params {
        if contains_any(&p.description, ALIAS_PHRASES) {
            return ConditionVerdict::Failed {
                why: format!("`{}` accepts more than one spelling of the same resource", p.name),
            };
        }
    }

    // Injectivity needs the identifier space pinned as well as the body
    // closed: an unconstrained path template can spell one resource many
    // ways just as an alias can.
    let closed = schema
        .and_then(|s| s.get("additionalProperties"))
        .and_then(Value::as_bool)
        == Some(false);
    let templated: Vec<&str> = path
        .split('/')
        .filter(|s| s.starts_with('{') && s.ends_with('}'))
        .collect();
    let all_constrained = templated.iter().all(|t| {
        let name = t.trim_matches(|c| c == '{' || c == '}').to_ascii_lowercase();
        params.iter().any(|p| {
            p.name == name
                && p.schema
                    .map(|s| s.get("pattern").is_some() || s.get("format").is_some())
                    .unwrap_or(false)
        })
    });

    if closed && all_constrained {
        ConditionVerdict::Satisfied
    } else if !closed {
        ConditionVerdict::Unknown {
            why: "the body schema admits additional properties, so two wire forms may parse to one document".into(),
        }
    } else {
        ConditionVerdict::Unknown {
            why: "a path parameter is unconstrained, so the identifier space is not pinned".into(),
        }
    }
}

fn judge_c3(method: &str, schema: Option<&Value>, params: &[Param]) -> ConditionVerdict {
    if IDEMPOTENT_METHODS.contains(&method) {
        return ConditionVerdict::Satisfied;
    }
    let required = required_names(schema);
    let in_body = properties(schema)
        .into_iter()
        .find(|(n, _)| IDEMPOTENCY_NAMES.contains(&n.as_str()));
    if let Some((name, _)) = in_body {
        return if required.contains(&name) {
            ConditionVerdict::Satisfied
        } else {
            ConditionVerdict::ClientDependent {
                how: format!("send the optional `{name}` on every request"),
            }
        };
    }
    match params.iter().find(|p| IDEMPOTENCY_NAMES.contains(&p.name.as_str())) {
        Some(p) if p.required => ConditionVerdict::Satisfied,
        Some(p) => ConditionVerdict::ClientDependent {
            how: format!("send the optional `{}` on every request", p.name),
        },
        None => ConditionVerdict::Failed {
            why: format!("{method} is not idempotent and the interface offers no idempotency key the client could supply"),
        },
    }
}

fn judge_c4(schema: Option<&Value>, params: &[Param]) -> ConditionVerdict {
    let required = required_names(schema);
    if let Some((name, _)) = properties(schema)
        .into_iter()
        .find(|(n, _)| PINNING_NAMES.contains(&n.as_str()))
    {
        return if required.contains(&name) {
            ConditionVerdict::Satisfied
        } else {
            ConditionVerdict::ClientDependent {
                how: format!("send the optional `{name}` precondition on every request"),
            }
        };
    }
    match params.iter().find(|p| PINNING_NAMES.contains(&p.name.as_str())) {
        Some(p) if p.required => ConditionVerdict::Satisfied,
        Some(p) => ConditionVerdict::ClientDependent {
            how: format!("send the optional `{}` precondition on every request", p.name),
        },
        // Not `Failed`: a specification cannot say whether the effect depends
        // on server state. Only a probe can, which is the methodological
        // crux of the whole empirical half of this paper.
        None => ConditionVerdict::Unknown {
            why: "no precondition mechanism is described, and a specification cannot say whether the effect depends on server state".into(),
        },
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write acceptance test 4**

Append to `tests/acceptance.rs`:

```rust
use transit::classify::{classify_spec, ConditionVerdict, Verdict};

fn classify_example(name: &str) -> transit::classify::Classification {
    let src = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("examples/{name}")).expect("example present");
    classify_spec(&src).expect("valid spec").into_iter().next().unwrap()
}

/// Acceptance test 4: `classify` on an ISO 20022 style spec returns
/// `Qualified`; on a Slack `chat.postMessage` style spec it returns
/// `Non-qualifying`, naming the specific conditions that failed.
#[test]
fn classify_separates_a_bindable_spec_from_an_unbindable_one() {
    let iso = classify_example("iso20022-pain001.openapi.json");
    assert_eq!(iso.verdict, Verdict::Qualified);
    assert!(iso.failing.is_empty(), "{:?}", iso.failing);
    for c in [Condition::C1, Condition::C2, Condition::C3, Condition::C4] {
        assert_eq!(iso.verdict_for(c), Some(&ConditionVerdict::Satisfied), "{c:?}");
    }

    let slack = classify_example("slack-chat-postmessage.openapi.json");
    assert_eq!(slack.verdict, Verdict::NonQualifying);
    // Naming the failing conditions is the requirement, not just the verdict.
    assert!(slack.failing.contains(&Condition::C1), "{:?}", slack.failing);
    assert!(slack.failing.contains(&Condition::C2), "{:?}", slack.failing);
    assert!(slack.failing.contains(&Condition::C3), "{:?}", slack.failing);

    // Every verdict here came from reading a document, and says so.
    assert_eq!(iso.evidence, Evidence::SpecDerived);
    assert_eq!(slack.evidence, Evidence::SpecDerived);
}

/// The middle of the taxonomy, and the S3 case, so the classifier is shown
/// to distinguish three outcomes rather than two.
#[test]
fn classify_places_the_conditional_and_qualified_middle_cases() {
    assert_eq!(
        classify_example("stripe-charges.openapi.json").verdict,
        Verdict::Conditional
    );
    assert_eq!(
        classify_example("s3-put-if-match.openapi.json").verdict,
        Verdict::Qualified
    );
}
```

Expected: ISO and S3 `Qualified`, Stripe `Conditional`, Slack `NonQualifying`.

If any of the four disagrees with the row the desk study assigned it, **write the disagreement down in the commit message and carry it into the paper** rather than adjusting the rules to match. A classifier tuned to reproduce a documentation review is no longer evidence about anything.

```bash
so a missing signal caps the result at Conditional instead of asserting a
```

### Task 11: `transit probe` and the authorization gate

**Files:**
- Create: `src/probe.rs`, `examples/targets.example.toml`
- Modify: `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`, `tests/acceptance.rs`, `README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `transport::{Http, RateLimiter, Transport}`, `fuzz::run_all`, `corpus::Evidence`.
- Produces: `transit::probe::{Targets, Target, Authorization, GateError, gate, load_targets, probe}`. `gate(&Targets, flag_given: bool) -> Result<(), GateError>` is the whole of the safety story and is tested independently of any network; `probe(&Target) -> Result<FuzzReport, ProbeError>` runs the checks against a live target. CLI: `transit probe --live --config <targets.toml> --i-am-authorized`, exiting **3** when the gate refuses.

**This gate is a hard requirement, not a nicety.** Unauthorized adversarial traffic to third-party production APIs is the one thing this repository must not enable. The gate is defence in depth rather than a guarantee — the README says plainly that the operator is responsible — but it must be impossible to reach the network path without passing through it.

Four independent conditions, every one of which must hold:

1. `--i-am-authorized` was given on the command line.
2. Every target declares `authorization = "own-account"` or `"written-permission"`.
3. Every target declares a non-empty `contact` that looks reachable.
4. The config parses with `deny_unknown_fields`, so `authorisation` misspelled is a loud error rather than a field that quietly is not there.

Create `src/probe.rs`:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const GOOD: &str = r#"
[[target]]
name = "my-own-sandbox"
base_url = "https://sandbox.example.com"
authorization = "own-account"
contact = "jim@quome.com"
rate_limit_per_minute = 6
"#;

    fn targets(src: &str) -> Targets {
        toml::from_str(src).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_well_formed_config_with_both_flags_passes() {
        assert!(gate(&targets(GOOD), true, true).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn each_missing_flag_is_reported_as_itself() {
        // Collapsing the two into one boolean made omitting `--live` report
        // that `--i-am-authorized` was missing — wrong, and the kind of wrong
        // that teaches an operator to add the flag that was not the problem.
        assert!(matches!(
            gate(&targets(GOOD), true, false),
            Err(GateError::FlagMissing)
        ));
        assert!(matches!(
            gate(&targets(GOOD), false, true),
            Err(GateError::LiveMissing)
        ));
        assert!(gate(&targets(GOOD), false, false).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_authorization_declaration_is_a_parse_error() {
        let src = GOOD.replace("authorization = \"own-account\"\n", "");
        assert!(toml::from_str::<Targets>(&src).is_err(), "there is no default");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_misspelled_authorization_key_is_rejected_rather_than_ignored() {
        // Without `deny_unknown_fields` this would parse as a target with no
        // authorization key at all — and the field it was meant to set has
        // no default, so the failure would at least be loud. This test pins
        // that it stays loud if a default is ever added.
        let src = GOOD.replace("authorization =", "authorisation =");
        assert!(toml::from_str::<Targets>(&src).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_or_implausible_contact_is_refused() {
        for bad in ["\"\"", "\"   \"", "\"me\""] {
            let src = GOOD.replace("\"jim@quome.com\"", bad);
            let t: Targets = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
            assert!(
                matches!(gate(&t, true, true), Err(GateError::UnusableContact { .. })),
                "contact {bad} should be refused"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_the_two_declared_authorization_values_parse() {
        let src = GOOD.replace("\"own-account\"", "\"i-guess-its-fine\"");
        assert!(toml::from_str::<Targets>(&src).is_err());
        let src = GOOD.replace("\"own-account\"", "\"written-permission\"");
        assert!(gate(&targets(&src), true, true).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_config_with_no_targets_is_refused_rather_than_vacuously_allowed() {
        assert!(matches!(gate(&targets(""), true, true), Err(GateError::NoTargets)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn one_bad_target_among_good_ones_refuses_the_whole_run() {
        let src = format!("{GOOD}\n[[target]]\nname = \"other\"\nbase_url = \"https://x.example\"\nauthorization = \"own-account\"\ncontact = \"\"\n");
        let t: Targets = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
        assert!(gate(&t, true, true).is_err(), "the gate is all-or-nothing");
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_gate_error_says_what_to_do_about_it() {
        // The refusal has to be an explanation, not a stack trace.
        for e in [
            GateError::FlagMissing,
            GateError::NoTargets,
            GateError::UnusableContact { name: "t".into() },
        ] {
            let msg = e.to_string();
            assert!(msg.len() > 40, "too terse: {msg}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_target_defaults_to_a_conservative_rate_limit() {
        let src = GOOD.replace("rate_limit_per_minute = 6\n", "");
        let t: Targets = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(t.target[0].rate_limit_per_minute, DEFAULT_RATE_PER_MINUTE);
        assert!(DEFAULT_RATE_PER_MINUTE <= 12, "the default must be gentle");
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/probe.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::Evidence;
use crate::fuzz::{run_all, FuzzReport};
use crate::transport::{Http, RateLimiter, TransportError};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::path::Path;

/// Ten requests a minute. Deliberately gentle: the default has to be one
/// nobody would need to think about before running against something real.
pub const DEFAULT_RATE_PER_MINUTE: u32 = 10;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum Authorization {
    /// The operator owns the account being probed.
    OwnAccount,
    /// The operator holds written permission from whoever does.
    WrittenPermission,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Target {
    pub name: String,
    pub base_url: String,
    /// No default, on purpose. An operator has to write this down.
    pub authorization: Authorization,
    /// Someone the probed party can reach if the probing is unwelcome.
    pub contact: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_rate")]
    pub rate_limit_per_minute: u32,
}

fn default_rate() -> u32 {
    DEFAULT_RATE_PER_MINUTE
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Targets {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub target: Vec<Target>,
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum GateError {
    #[error(
        "refusing to probe: `--i-am-authorized` was not given. This command sends \
         adversarial traffic to whatever it is pointed at, so it will not run until \
         you state, on the command line, that you are authorized to do that."
    )]
    FlagMissing,
    #[error(
        "refusing to probe: `--live` was not given. Nothing reaches the network \
         without it, and it is separate from `--i-am-authorized` so that neither \
         flag can be mistaken for the other."
    )]
    LiveMissing,
    #[error(
        "refusing to probe: the config names no targets. An empty target list is \
         almost always a mistake in the config rather than a request to do nothing, \
         so it is refused rather than silently satisfied."
    )]
    NoTargets,
    #[error(
        "refusing to probe `{name}`: `contact` must be an address the probed party \
         can actually reach — an email address or a URL. Whoever receives this \
         traffic needs a way to tell you to stop."
    )]
    UnusableContact { name: String },
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ProbeError {
    #[error("could not read {path}: {source}")]
    Io {
        path: String,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },
    #[error("could not parse {path}: {source}")]
    Parse {
        path: String,
        #[source]
        source: toml::de::Error,
    },
    #[error(transparent)]
    Gate(#[from] GateError),
    #[error(transparent)]
    Transport(#[from] TransportError),
}

/// The whole safety story, in one function with no I/O in it, so it can be
/// tested exhaustively without a network.
pub fn gate(targets: &Targets, live: bool, authorized: bool) -> Result<(), GateError> {
    // Checked separately. Collapsing them into one boolean made omitting
    // `--live` report that `--i-am-authorized` was missing, which is both
    // wrong and the kind of wrong that teaches an operator to add the flag
    // that was not the problem.
    if !live {
        return Err(GateError::LiveMissing);
    }
    if !authorized {
        return Err(GateError::FlagMissing);
    }
    if targets.target.is_empty() {
        return Err(GateError::NoTargets);
    }
    for t in &targets.target {
        let c = t.contact.trim();
        // Not validation for its own sake: an unreachable contact means the
        // probed party has no way to object.
        if c.is_empty() || !(c.contains('@') || c.starts_with("http")) {
            return Err(GateError::UnusableContact { name: t.name.clone() });
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn load_targets(path: &Path) -> Result<Targets, ProbeError> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|source| ProbeError::Io {
        path: path.display().to_string(),
        source,
    })?;
    toml::from_str(&text).map_err(|source| ProbeError::Parse {
        path: path.display().to_string(),
        source,
    })
}

/// Run the adversarial checks against one live target. Only reachable after
/// `gate` has returned `Ok`.
///
/// **A limitation to state before anyone reads a result from this.** The
/// checks in `fuzz` address this repository's own routes — `/v1/transfer`,
/// `/v1/orders`, `/v1/users/me` — and parse a response shaped like the
/// hostile server's. Against a real API every request 404s, every check
/// returns nothing, and a naive reading of the output is "no findings" when
/// the truth is "no check applied". Probing a real endpoint needs per-target
/// route and payload configuration, which is **not built**.
///
/// So the report distinguishes the two. If no check got a response the target
/// recognised, `checks_applicable` is false and the summary says the checks
/// did not apply rather than that the endpoint is clean.
pub fn probe(t: &Target) -> Result<FuzzReport, ProbeError> {
    let mut http = Http {
        base: t.base_url.clone(),
        evidence: Evidence::LiveProbed,
        limiter: RateLimiter::per_minute(t.rate_limit_per_minute),
    };
    let mut report = run_all(&mut http, &format!("live:{}", t.name))?;
    // One cheap request decides it: if the endpoint does not know the route
    // the checks are built around, the checks did not run in any real sense.
    let probe_route = http.send(&crate::transport::Request {
        method: "GET".into(),
        path: "/v1/state".into(),
        headers: vec![],
        body: String::new(),
    })?;
    report.checks_applicable = probe_route.status != 404;
    Ok(report)
}
```

Update `src/lib.rs` to add `pub mod probe;`.

```toml
# Copy this file, edit it, and keep your copy out of version control.
#
# `transit probe` sends adversarial traffic. It runs only when this file
# declares, per target, that you are authorized — and only when you also
# pass --i-am-authorized on the command line. Both are required. Neither is
# a substitute for actually being authorized: the gate is defence in depth,
# and the operator is responsible for what this command sends.

[[target]]
name = "my-own-sandbox"
base_url = "https://sandbox.example.com"

# One of "own-account" or "written-permission". There is no third value and
# no default.
authorization = "own-account"

# An address the probed party can use to tell you to stop. Required.
contact = "you@example.com"

# Requests per minute. Defaults to 10 if omitted.
rate_limit_per_minute = 6
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Add the CLI subcommand, exiting 3 when refused**

Add to `enum Cmd`:

```rust
    /// Probe live endpoints. Gated: see `examples/targets.example.toml`.
    Probe {
        /// Required. Present so that no accidental invocation reaches the
        /// network.
        #[arg(long)]
        live: bool,
        #[arg(long)]
        config: PathBuf,
        /// Required. The gate refuses without it and explains why.
        #[arg(long)]
        i_am_authorized: bool,
        #[arg(long, default_value = "text")]
        format: Format,
    },
```

And to the `match`:

```rust
        Cmd::Probe { live, config, i_am_authorized, format } => {
            let targets = transit::probe::load_targets(&config)?;
            if let Err(e) = transit::probe::gate(&targets, live, i_am_authorized) {
                eprintln!("{e}");
                // Exit code 3 is reserved for this and nothing else, so a
                // caller can distinguish "refused" from "found something".
                return Ok(ExitCode::from(3));
            }
            let mut any = false;
            for t in &targets.target {
                let report = transit::probe::probe(t)?;
                any |= !report.findings.is_empty();
                if format == Format::Json {
                    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&report)?);
                } else {
                    println!("\n{} ({})", t.name, t.base_url);
                    println!("  evidence: {}", report.evidence.caveat());
                    for f in &report.findings {
                        println!("  {} [{}]", f.title, f.condition.title());
                    }
                }
            }
            Ok(if any { ExitCode::from(1) } else { ExitCode::SUCCESS })
        }
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Write acceptance test 5**

Append to `tests/acceptance.rs`:

```rust
use std::process::Command;

/// Acceptance test 5: `probe` exits non-zero with an explanatory message
/// when the authorization declaration is absent.
///
/// Driven through the real binary rather than through `gate` directly,
/// because the requirement is about what the *command* does. A library
/// function that refuses correctly while the CLI proceeds anyway would pass
/// a unit test and fail the requirement.
#[test]
fn probe_refuses_and_exits_three_without_authorization() {
    let bin = env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_transit");
    let cfg = "examples/targets.example.toml";

    // No --i-am-authorized.
    let out = Command::new(bin)
        .args(["probe", "--live", "--config", cfg])
        .output()
        .expect("the binary runs");
    assert_eq!(out.status.code(), Some(3), "exit code 3 is reserved for refusal");
    let msg = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
    assert!(msg.contains("--i-am-authorized"), "the refusal must explain itself: {msg}");
    assert!(msg.contains("refusing to probe"), "{msg}");

    // A config whose target omits the authorization declaration must not
    // even parse, so the run stops before anything is sent.
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("transit-acceptance");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
    let path = dir.join("no-authorization.toml");
    std::fs::write(
        &path,
        "[[target]]\nname = \"t\"\nbase_url = \"https://example.com\"\ncontact = \"a@b.c\"\n",
    )
    .unwrap();
    let out = Command::new(bin)
        .args(["probe", "--live", "--i-am-authorized", "--config"])
        .arg(&path)
        .output()
        .expect("the binary runs");
    let msg = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
    // The design's requirement is a non-zero exit *with an explanatory
    // message*, so assert the message, not just the code.
    assert!(
        matches!(out.status.code(), Some(2) | Some(3)),
        "expected a refusal, got {:?}: {msg}",
        out.status.code()
    );
    assert!(msg.contains("authorization"), "the refusal must name the field: {msg}");
    assert!(msg.contains("no-authorization.toml"), "and the file: {msg}");
    // Exit 2 rather than 3 here is deliberate and stricter than the gate: an
    // undeclared target fails at parse time, before the gate is reached and
    // before anything could possibly be sent. Code 3 stays reserved for a
    // config that parsed and was then refused.
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Add the gate to the README**

Append to `README.md`, above the licence section:

```markdown
## Live probing is gated

`transit probe` is the only command that touches a third-party network, and
it will not run unless **all** of the following hold:

1. `--live` and `--i-am-authorized` are both given on the command line.
2. Every target in the config declares `authorization = "own-account"` or
   `"written-permission"`. There is no default and no third value.
3. Every target declares a `contact` the probed party could use to tell you
   to stop.
4. The config parses with unknown fields rejected, so a misspelled key is a
   loud error rather than a setting that quietly is not there.

It refuses with exit code **3** and an explanation otherwise, and it is
rate-limited to 10 requests a minute by default.

`transit fuzz --target` is **not** gated, so it refuses any URL that is not
loopback and points you here instead. That is what keeps the sentence above
true: `fuzz` sends the same adversarial traffic, including a deliberate
double execution, and it labels its findings as coming from this
repository's hostile endpoint — a label that would be a lie about a
stranger's server.

This is defence in depth, not a guarantee. **The operator is responsible for
what this command sends.** Every other command in this tool runs against the
hostile endpoint in this repository, offline and with no credentials, which
is where the paper's reproductions come from.
```

### Task 12: The desk survey as a hypothesis, results generation, and the no-panic guarantee

**Files:**
- Create: `src/survey.rs`, `survey/desk-study.toml`, `tests/robustness.rs`, `scripts/regen-results.sh`, `results/README.md`
- Modify: `src/differential.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/bin/transit.rs`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything above.
- Produces: `transit::survey::{Survey, Source, Row, DeskVerdict, load, SurveyError}` with `Survey::evidence() -> Evidence`, `Survey::banner() -> String`, `Survey::counts() -> Vec<(DeskVerdict, usize)>`; `transit::differential::run_in_crate_only() -> Run`. CLI: `transit survey <file>`, and `transit differential --in-crate-only`. `results/` committed, regenerated by CI.

The 24-endpoint table is the single most quotable thing in the desk study and the single most dangerous. It is a documentation review. The module's job is to make it **impossible to print without saying so**, and impossible for a row to claim live verification it has not had.

Committed results split in two, for a reason worth stating. A cross-language measurement is a property of an environment: rerun it on a host with different runtime versions and it legitimately differs. So `results/differential-incrate.json` — built from decoders that ship in this crate, identical on every host — is diff-gated in CI, and `results/differential-host.json` carries its environment inline and is regenerated but not gated. What is gated across environments is the *claim*, asserted in `tests/acceptance.rs`, not the bytes.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing survey test**

Create `src/survey.rs`:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const MIN: &str = r#"
source = "documentation"
verified_live = false
note = "n"
[[endpoint]]
sector = "s"
provider = "p"
endpoint = "POST /x"
c1 = "fails"
c2 = "fails"
c3 = "fails"
c4 = "fails"
hypothesis = "non-qualifying"
"#;

    #[test]
    fn a_documentation_survey_is_always_desk_study_evidence() {
        let s: Survey = toml::from_str(MIN).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s.evidence(), Evidence::DeskStudy);
    }

    #[test]
    fn declaring_a_live_source_is_not_evidence_of_one() {
        // The hole the first invariant leaves: `source = "live-probe"` plus
        // `verified_live = true` would otherwise load cleanly and print
        // "MEASURED against live endpoints", with no probe anywhere.
        let src = MIN
            .replace("source = \"documentation\"", "source = \"live-probe\"")
            .replace("verified_live = false", "verified_live = true");
        let s: Survey = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(s.validate(), Err(SurveyError::RowWithoutEvidence { .. })));
        assert_eq!(s.evidence(), Evidence::LiveProbed, "before validation, it would claim this");
        // With a report named, it is allowed — and only then.
        let src = format!("{src}probe_report = \"results/probe-p.json\"\n");
        let s: Survey = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
        assert!(s.validate().is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_documentation_source_cannot_claim_live_verification() {
        // The one invariant that matters. A row promoted to LiveProbed
        // without a probe is the failure mode this whole module exists to
        // prevent, so it is refused at load time rather than at print time.
        let src = MIN.replace("verified_live = false", "verified_live = true");
        let s: Survey = toml::from_str(&src).unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(s.validate(), Err(SurveyError::UnearnedVerification)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_banner_names_the_sample_and_refuses_the_word_result() {
        let s: Survey = toml::from_str(MIN).unwrap();
        let b = s.banner();
        assert!(b.contains("HYPOTHESIS"), "{b}");
        assert!(b.contains("not a population estimate"), "{b}");
        assert!(b.contains("convenience sample"), "{b}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_shipped_survey_has_the_twenty_four_rows_the_desk_study_classified() {
        let s = load(std::path::Path::new("survey/desk-study.toml")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s.endpoint.len(), 24);
        assert_eq!(s.source, Source::Documentation);
        assert!(!s.verified_live);
        assert!(s.validate().is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_shipped_counts_match_the_desk_study_split() {
        let s = load(std::path::Path::new("survey/desk-study.toml")).unwrap();
        let counts = s.counts();
        let get = |v: DeskVerdict| counts.iter().find(|(k, _)| *k == v).map(|(_, n)| *n).unwrap_or(0);
        // 4 / 7 / 13, from counting the desk study's own 24-row table.
        //
        // Its prose says 4 / 6 / 14 — "25.0% (6 out of 24)" and "58.3% (14 out
        // of 24)" — and P02 inherits those figures. The table does not support
        // them: Square is a Conditional row the 25% count omits and the 58%
        // count then absorbs. The rows are right and the arithmetic over them
        // is wrong.
        //
        // Transcribe the table. Do not adjust a row to reach 14. This is the
        // "no number in the paper can drift from the code that produced it"
        // discipline doing its job on its first use, and the correction is a
        // small result of its own.
        assert_eq!(get(DeskVerdict::Qualified), 4);
        assert_eq!(get(DeskVerdict::Conditional), 7);
        assert_eq!(get(DeskVerdict::NonQualifying), 13);
        assert_eq!(s.endpoint.len(), 4 + 7 + 13);
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_row_names_a_provider_and_an_endpoint() {
        let s = load(std::path::Path::new("survey/desk-study.toml")).unwrap();
        for r in &s.endpoint {
            assert!(!r.provider.trim().is_empty());
            assert!(!r.endpoint.trim().is_empty(), "{}", r.provider);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_malformed_survey_errors_rather_than_panicking() {
        for bad in ["", "source = 5", "source = \"telepathy\"\nverified_live = false\nnote = \"n\""] {
            assert!(toml::from_str::<Survey>(bad).is_err(), "{bad:?}");
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Prepend to `src/survey.rs`:

```rust
use crate::corpus::Evidence;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::path::Path;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum Source {
    /// Read off published documentation. The only source this file has.
    Documentation,
    /// Measured against running endpoints under the authorization gate.
    LiveProbe,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum DeskVerdict {
    Qualified,
    Conditional,
    NonQualifying,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum RowVerdict {
    Satisfied,
    ClientDependent,
    Fails,
    Unknown,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Row {
    pub sector: String,
    pub provider: String,
    pub endpoint: String,
    pub c1: RowVerdict,
    pub c2: RowVerdict,
    pub c3: RowVerdict,
    pub c4: RowVerdict,
    pub hypothesis: DeskVerdict,
    /// The `results/` file holding the probe run this row came from. Absent
    /// for every documentation-derived row, and **required** before the file
    /// may declare itself live — otherwise "measured" is a claim anyone can
    /// make by editing two lines of TOML.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub probe_report: Option<String>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Survey {
    pub source: Source,
    pub verified_live: bool,
    pub note: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub endpoint: Vec<Row>,
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum SurveyError {
    #[error("could not read {path}: {source}")]
    Io {
        path: String,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },
    #[error("could not parse {path}: {source}")]
    Parse {
        path: String,
        #[source]
        source: toml::de::Error,
    },
    #[error(
        "this survey declares `source = \"documentation\"` and `verified_live = true`, \
         which cannot both be so. A documentation review is not a measurement, and \
         nothing in this repository may present one as the other."
    )]
    UnearnedVerification,
    #[error(
        "this survey claims live verification, but the row for `{provider}` names no \
         `probe_report`. A row is measured when a probe run says so, not when the \
         file says so."
    )]
    RowWithoutEvidence { provider: String },
}

impl Survey {
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), SurveyError> {
        if self.verified_live && self.source != Source::LiveProbe {
            return Err(SurveyError::UnearnedVerification);
        }
        // Declaring `source = "live-probe"` is not evidence either. Every row
        // has to name the probe run it came from, so the promotion path is a
        // measurement rather than two edited lines of TOML.
        if self.verified_live {
            for r in &self.endpoint {
                if r.probe_report.as_deref().unwrap_or("").trim().is_empty() {
                    return Err(SurveyError::RowWithoutEvidence {
                        provider: r.provider.clone(),
                    });
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// `DeskStudy` unless the rows were genuinely probed. There is no path
    /// through this function that returns anything stronger by accident.
    pub fn evidence(&self) -> Evidence {
        if self.verified_live && self.source == Source::LiveProbe {
            Evidence::LiveProbed
        } else {
            Evidence::DeskStudy
        }
    }

    /// Printed above every rendering of this table, in every format.
    pub fn banner(&self) -> String {
        if self.evidence() == Evidence::LiveProbed {
            return format!("MEASURED against live endpoints. n = {}.", self.endpoint.len());
        }
        format!(
            "HYPOTHESIS, NOT DATA. These {} classifications are derived from published \
             documentation and have not been measured against a running endpoint. C1 and \
             C4 are properties of an implementation, which no document reveals. The sample \
             was chosen for sector coverage, so it is a convenience sample and the shares \
             below are not a population estimate. Verifying these rows by live probing is \
             work this repository has not done.",
            self.endpoint.len()
        )
    }

    pub fn counts(&self) -> Vec<(DeskVerdict, usize)> {
        [DeskVerdict::Qualified, DeskVerdict::Conditional, DeskVerdict::NonQualifying]
            .into_iter()
            .map(|v| (v, self.endpoint.iter().filter(|r| r.hypothesis == v).count()))
            .collect()
    }
}

pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Survey, SurveyError> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|source| SurveyError::Io {
        path: path.display().to_string(),
        source,
    })?;
    let s: Survey = toml::from_str(&text).map_err(|source| SurveyError::Parse {
        path: path.display().to_string(),
        source,
    })?;
    s.validate()?;
    Ok(s)
}
```
